# Claims register

Generated from [`register.json`](register.json) by [`check_register.py`](check_register.py); edit the JSON, not this file.

Every row is one claim a preliminary source makes, with a short verbatim anchor proving the source makes it. *Status* records what the lab has done with the claim; nothing reaches the talk with a status other than `verified`, `corrected`, `lab_supported` or `stipulation`.

Status counts: corrected 351, lab_contradicted 127, lab_supported 122, needs_primary 66, out_of_scope 343, refuted 82, stipulation 160, unverifiable 54, verified 153 — 1458 rows.

## S1 — Peirce's logical argument (82)

| id | source | kind | claim | cited work | status | note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TR-S1-001 | D1 | definition | Peirce's Reduction Thesis: monadic and dyadic relations can never generate genuine triadic relations. |  | corrected | → The negative clause is a theorem only in PAL without teridentity. PAL here means product, join of two places, complement and permutation, with id3 as the only built-in constant. For any set A with \|A\|≥2, the relations generated from all unary and binary relations do not include id3 (Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2006, Thm 2; Hereth & Pöschel 2011). Burch 1991 proved it only under his juxtaposition restriction. The clause fails under pp/relational-algebra closure, where =3 = ∆(ζ(= × =)) (Dau & Hereth Correia 2006, p.108), and under pairing or domain extension. Peirce argued the clause; he did not prove it ('proves' in 031 is wrong). HP call the theorem 'a mathematical version' of the Thirdness claim, so 054's 'non-reducibility of Thirdness' goes beyond the result. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] Standard statement of the negative clause; 'never' holds only under Peirce/Burch's operations (no pairing/domain enlargement) — Löwenheim 1915 and Quine 1954 reduce all relations to dyads with pairing. |
| TR-S1-002 | D1 | definition | Second half of the Reduction Thesis: triadic relations suffice to synthesize all polyadic relations of any arity. |  | corrected | → In restriction-free PAL the positive clause is proved as follows. Finite relations are built from ternaries using teridentity and complement, where complement is needed for union. Infinite relations also need the ternary graph of a bijection A×A→A, which for arbitrary infinite A requires the axiom of choice (Hereth & Pöschel 2011 §3, note 2). Burch obtains the clause on finite domains by enlarging the domain through hypostatic abstraction. Herzberger's construction needs \|A\| at least \|R\| (Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2004, Remark 15). In pp terms, n≥4 reduces to ternaries on every domain (koshkin2025completeness). 'Universally sufficient' is therefore true only relative to a stated operation set. The n≥3 vs n≥4 question in 050 is moot. [hereth2011logic] Positive clause needs teridentity/hypostatic abstraction as allowed operations; 'universally sufficient' is Burch's theorem within PAL, not a general fact. |
| TR-S1-003 | D1 | historical | Peirce's logic of relatives and Beta Existential Graphs evaluated relations from a generative, internal standpoint. |  | unverifiable | → None of the four PAL papers read (HCP 2004, HCP 2006, Dau & HC 2006, HP 2011) contrasts a 'generative, internal standpoint' with a closure view. The nearest wording is Burch's 'unitary logical vision' as quoted by HP 2011, p.1. Drop the phrase or present it as the talk's own gloss. [hereth2011logic] Characterization appears to be Gemini's gloss; no primary locus given. Unsafe to use without a secondary source that says it. |
| TR-S1-004 | D1 | definition | Peirce's question: over a fixed universe, can higher-arity relations be generated from lower-arity ones by basic operations? |  | corrected | → The fixed-universe generation question is Hereth & Pöschel's algebraic reformulation (HCP 2004; HP 2011 p.6), not Peirce's own wording. Dau & Hereth Correia 2006 (p.106) say the thesis is not stated explicitly in Peirce. [hereth2011logic] Do not present as 'Peirce's question' verbatim; it is the modern algebraic reformulation. |
| TR-S1-005 | D1 | logical | Chaining R(x,y) and S(y,z) via the relative product ∃y[R∧S] requires y to branch into two predicate locations. |  | refuted | → The relative product ∃y[R(x,y)∧S(y,z)] is juxtaposition followed by one join of two hooks, ρ∘σ = δ_{m,m+1}(ρ×σ), with no teridentity (HCP 2004, Thm 9 proof). Burch's join2 is the same operation (HP 2011 p.6), and Dau & HC 2006 p.115 define ◦ as δ(R×S). The variable y has degree 2, a plain line of identity. PAL needs teridentity for conjunction on shared variables (HCP 2004 Fig. 4: ρ∩σ is built with two id3 nodes) and for identifying coordinates, i.e. wherever one variable fills three or more places. (Symbols in the quotation are normalized from a garbled text layer.) [herethcorreia2004power] Likely wrong: in ∃y[R(x,y)∧S(y,z)] y occurs twice, which is a single line of identity joining two hooks (degree 2), not a branch. Branching (teridentity) is needed only when a variable fills three or more places. Peirce's own CP 1.363 point is that composing dyads yields a dyad. |
| TR-S1-006 | D1 | definition | In Existential Graphs the branch is a degree-3 ligature vertex, a teridentity node. |  | corrected | → The existing correction stands. Sowa's 'point of teridentity' block quote has no Peirce locus: it carries no citation, and Sowa inserts '[Figure 6 shows]'. His only attribution is the p. 993 rule that uncited Peirce quotations come from NEM 3:162–169, a range that was not checked. Quote it only as Peirce quoted by Sowa (2018, p. 996). 'Fundamental' does not occur in Sowa's pp. 995–997, and 'join-irreducible' has no source. [sowa2018reasoning] A branch point of a ligature is indeed read as teridentity, but in this source it is attached to the relative-product case (row 005), where no branch occurs. |
| TR-S1-007 | D1 | logical | Graphs with vertex degree at most 2 yield only unbranched paths and disconnected cycles. |  | verified | → As graph theory the claim is true: a graph of maximum degree 2 is a disjoint union of paths and cycles. None of the sources read states the paths/cycles sentence itself. The literature's formal counterparts are HCP 2004 Remark 14 (valency ≤2 in positive PAL without id3), HCP 2006 Lemma 1 (no t-connected set of more than 2 places) and Herzberger's valency rule, reported secondhand. Cite those for the logic and graph theory for the sentence. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] True as graph theory. Lab consistent but not evidence for the theorem: thinkers-peirce-h1 expect 'max cause-side adicity over all=2'. Note lab also has pure cycles as Φ=2.0 wholes, so 'only cycles' is not 'only trivial'. |
| TR-S1-008 | D1 | argument_step | Quine's formulation falls into the trap Peirce identified in his analysis of dual relatives. |  | refuted | → The attribution to Peirce is anachronistic: Peirce died in 1914. His objection targets Kempe: CP 3.424, 'fails to afford any formal representation of the manner in which this abstract idea is derived from the concrete ideas', as quoted by Koshkin 2022, not 2024, p. 3. Koshkin p. 4 reports that it 'is often pressed against pairing reductions a la Löwenheim and Quine as well' and warns it is 'a double edged sword'. The use/mention diagnosis of dyadic reductions, which TR-S1-015 attributes to Peirce, is Koshkin's own application (p. 2) of Peirce's remark about dyadic algebra (CP 8.331). [quine1954reduction] Peirce could not have analyzed Quine; the argument that Quine's reduction presupposes teridentity belongs to later commentators. 'Dual relatives' analysis (CP 1.363) must be cited exactly. |
| TR-S1-009 | D1 | quotation | Peirce quoted (CP 8.331): 'the very triadic relations which it does not recognize it itself employs'. | Peirce, Collected Papers 8.331 | corrected | → The locus is right but the wording is not. CP 8.331 and Wiener 1958 read "the very triadic relations which it does not recognize, it does itself employ", and Hardwick 1977 prints the same words without the comma. No printing has "it itself employs". The sentence is about Peirce's own algebra of dyadic relations, and Kempe is not mentioned. CP's "[my] algebra" is an editorial substitution; the letter reads "that algebra of dyadic relations" (Hardwick, Wiener). The wording was checked in scan OCR of the printed CP 8 and both editions. [peirce1904letter] Verify wording and locus against CP 8.331 before quoting; context is Kempe, not Quine. |
| TR-S1-010 | D1 | logical | FOL syntax hides teridentity by letting one variable recur in several argument positions. |  | corrected | → Dau & Hereth Correia locate the hidden teridentity in identification of coordinates: =3 = ∆(ζ(= × =)) in relational algebra (p.108). It is hidden when one variable occurs in three or more argument places. Two occurrences is a plain two-hook join. Sowa 2018 Table 3 illustrates the three-place case. [dau2006instances] Holds only when a variable occurs in three or more argument places; two occurrences is a plain identity line (degree 2). |
| TR-S1-011 | D1 | definition | Monadic tier: unary relations match Firstness (immediate quality) and Shannon entropy. |  | corrected | → The Peircean half is supported. Firstness is quality and possibility (CP 1.25), the pure monad is "a pure nature, or quality" (CP 1.303), and the first is "present and immediate" (CP 1.357). The pairing with Shannon or marginal entropy H(X), "baseline noise" and "uncoupled uncertainty" has no Peircean locus; it is the sources' analogy. "Ground" was not checked. [peirce1931papers] Firstness-monad link is Peircean; the Shannon entropy mapping is Gemini's analogy, not in Peirce and not in the lab record. |
| TR-S1-012 | D1 | definition | Dyadic tier: binary relations formalize pairwise coupling, i.e. Peircean Secondness. |  | corrected | → Peirce ties the dyad to Secondness himself (CP 8.330). Secondness as resistance and reaction is in CP 1.324, pairs and causation in CP 1.325, and "suffers and yet resists" in CP 1.358. "Relate" is his vocabulary (CP 8.330: "The Second, or Relate"). Three things are the sources' framing, not Peirce's: "formalize pairwise coupling", the alignment with equality and composition operations, and Shannon mutual information. [peirce1931papers] Standard gloss; 'formalize pairwise coupling' is modern framing. |
| TR-S1-013 | D1 | definition | Teridentity introduces the branch, binding three coordinates: x = y = z. |  | corrected | → The existing correction stands. Sowa's 'point of teridentity' block quote has no Peirce locus: it carries no citation, and Sowa inserts '[Figure 6 shows]'. His only attribution is the p. 993 rule that uncited Peirce quotations come from NEM 3:162–169, a range that was not checked. Quote it only as Peirce quoted by Sowa (2018, p. 996). 'Fundamental' does not occur in Sowa's pp. 995–997, and 'join-irreducible' has no source. [sowa2018reasoning] Definition of teridentity as x=y=z is standard; 'introduces the algebraic branch' is Burch's reading. |
| TR-S1-014 | D1 | definition | Abstract: Peirce's Reduction Thesis: triads not decomposable into dyads; triads generate all polyads. |  | corrected | → The two-clause statement is Burch's, in PAL (p. 106). -078's 'dyads can never generate triads' is false under relational-algebra (pp) closure, where =3 = ∆(ζ(= × =)) (p. 108), and needs the scope 'in PAL without teridentity'. [dau2006instances] Fine as a summary; attach operation-relativity caveat (Skidmore 1971 gerrymandering worry). |

For a one-sentence statement in Burch's own words cite burch2021sep §14: 'all relations … may be constructed from triadic relations alone, whereas monadic and dyadic relations alone are not sufficient to allow the construction of even a single “non-degenerate” (that is: non-Cartesian-factorable) triadic relation.' | TR-S1-019 | D4 | definition | Genuine triadicity is identified with Peircean Thirdness and with the teridentity relation =_3. |  | refuted | → The PAL authors call the teridentity theorem 'a mathematical version' of the Thirdness claim. They do not identify Thirdness with =3. Burch's criterion, known only from Koshkin's transcription of p.117, locates Thirdness in operations that presuppose a non-degenerate triad. Say 'teridentity is the PAL witness of genuine triadicity', not 'Thirdness is =3'. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] Conflation: teridentity is one logical relation (the degenerate-looking identity triad); Thirdness is a category (mediation, law). Peirce ties them, but identifying them outright needs a textual warrant. |

Drop 'join-irreducible' (041, 046), which no PAL source uses. [hereth2011logic] Definition correct; 'join-irreducible' in the anchor is unsourced (see row 035). |
| TR-S1-042 | G1 t1 | definition | Monad maps to Peirce's Firstness, entropy and baseline noise: unary selection, uncoupled potentiality. |  | corrected | → The Peircean half is supported. Firstness is quality and possibility (CP 1.25), the pure monad is "a pure nature, or quality" (CP 1.303), and the first is "present and immediate" (CP 1.357). The pairing with Shannon or marginal entropy H(X), "baseline noise" and "uncoupled uncertainty" has no Peircean locus; it is the sources' analogy. "Ground" was not checked. [peirce1931papers] 'entropy' and 'baseline noise' are Gemini analogies. |
| TR-S1-043 | G1 t1 | definition | Thirdness is glossed as teridentity, mediation, representation and genuine causal integration. |  | lab_contradicted | Equating Thirdness/teridentity with 'genuine causal integration' (and with XOR gates) is contradicted: integration (Φ>0) arises without any three-term fact, and three-term facts occur in Φ=0 systems. The mediation/representation gloss of Thirdness is separately needs_primary (CP 1.337ff). |
| TR-S1-044 | G1 t1 | logical | Relations of arity greater than 3 require no primitives beyond triads (the reducibility half of the thesis). |  | corrected | → In restriction-free PAL the positive clause is proved as follows. Finite relations are built from ternaries using teridentity and complement, where complement is needed for union. Infinite relations also need the ternary graph of a bijection A×A→A, which for arbitrary infinite A requires the axiom of choice (Hereth & Pöschel 2011 §3, note 2). Burch obtains the clause on finite domains by enlarging the domain through hypostatic abstraction. Herzberger's construction needs \|A\| at least \|R\| (Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2004, Remark 15). In pp terms, n≥4 reduces to ternaries on every domain (koshkin2025completeness). 'Universally sufficient' is therefore true only relative to a stated operation set. The n≥3 vs n≥4 question in 050 is moot. [hereth2011logic] Lab H5 (58 of 60 two-input forms reach adicity 4, thinkers-peirce-h5 'CONFIRMED') is an IIT existence result, not evidence for the algebraic theorem. 'subcubic hypergraph networks' wording needs a source. |
| TR-S1-045 | G3 t1 | definition | Glosses Peirce's Thirdness as genuine mediation, representation and lawful integration. |  | corrected | → Mediation and representation are Peirce's glosses of Thirdness (CP 1.337, 1.339, 1.356; CP 3.422: "thirdness, or mediation"), and so is law (CP 1.26; CP 8.331: "possessor according to Law"). "Lawful integration" is not his phrase and must not be equated with IIT integration or Phi. [peirce1931papers] 'lawful integration' must not be read as IIT integration (lab paper.md l.23-25 separates the two). Surrounding context is XAI framing. |
| TR-S1-046 | G5 t1 | definition | Defines teridentity as =_3 = {(x,x,x) \| x ∈ X} and calls it join-irreducible. |  | verified | → The definition is correct and standard. Drop 'join-irreducible' (041, 046), which no PAL source uses. [hereth2011logic] 'join-irreducible' unsourced (see row 035). |
| TR-S1-047 | G6 t4 | definition | Peirce's Reduction Thesis: ternary relations suffice to construct all relations, but unary and binary relations do not. | Dau & Hereth Correia (2006) | corrected | → The two-clause statement is Burch's, in PAL (p. 106). -078's 'dyads can never generate triads' is false under relational-algebra (pp) closure, where =3 = ∆(ζ(= × =)) (p. 108), and needs the scope 'in PAL without teridentity'. [dau2006instances] Check exact wording of Dau & Hereth Correia's statement before attributing. |
| TR-S1-048 | G6 t4 | logical | Peirce's Reduction Thesis: genuine triadic relations cannot be synthesized from dyadic relations without artificial domain expansion; the proofs confirm it. |  | corrected | → Both classical reductions change the domain. Löwenheim moves to pairs of old elements, which is n² for n individuals. Quine closes the universe under {x, y}. Holding the universe fixed is not enough for irreducibility. Koshkin (arXiv 2406.14094, Cor. 1) shows that 'Any n-ary with n ≥ 3 on an infinite domain reduces to a projoin of n binaries'. Irreducibility needs restricted operations as well: PAL without teridentity, or Herzberger's no-branching valency rule. Koshkin also shows it on small finite domains. 'The proofs confirm it' holds only inside those operation sets. 'Artificial' is Gemini's word. [lowenheim1915moglichkeiten] 'the proofs confirm it' holds only within PAL-type operations; 'artificial domain expansion' refers to Quine/Löwenheim pairing and is a characterization that needs a source. |
| TR-S1-049 | G6 t4 | rhetorical | Presupposes a 'four-tier Peircean categorical matrix' onto which ΦID proofs can be mapped. |  | out_of_scope | ΦID mapping onto a 'Peircean matrix' is Gemini's framing; Peirce has three categories, not four. |
| TR-S1-050 | G6 t6 | definition | PRT, positive half: every relation of adicity n >= 3 can be constructed from monadic, dyadic and triadic relations. | Burch (1991) | corrected | → In restriction-free PAL the positive clause is proved as follows. Finite relations are built from ternaries using teridentity and complement, where complement is needed for union. Infinite relations also need the ternary graph of a bijection A×A→A, which for arbitrary infinite A requires the axiom of choice (Hereth & Pöschel 2011 §3, note 2). Burch obtains the clause on finite domains by enlarging the domain through hypostatic abstraction. Herzberger's construction needs \|A\| at least \|R\| (Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2004, Remark 15). In pp terms, n≥4 reduces to ternaries on every domain (koshkin2025completeness). 'Universally sufficient' is therefore true only relative to a stated operation set. The n≥3 vs n≥4 question in 050 is moot. [hereth2011logic] Burch's formulation; check whether he states 'n >= 3' or 'n >= 4'. |
| TR-S1-051 | G6 t6 | definition | PRT, negative half: genuine triadic relations, specifically teridentity (=3), cannot be reduced to unary and binary relations alone. | Burch (1991) | corrected | → The negative clause is a theorem only in PAL without teridentity. PAL here means product, join of two places, complement and permutation, with id3 as the only built-in constant. For any set A with \|A\|≥2, the relations generated from all unary and binary relations do not include id3 (Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2006, Thm 2; Hereth & Pöschel 2011). Burch 1991 proved it only under his juxtaposition restriction. The clause fails under pp/relational-algebra closure, where =3 = ∆(ζ(= × =)) (Dau & Hereth Correia 2006, p.108), and under pairing or domain extension. Peirce argued the clause; he did not prove it ('proves' in 031 is wrong). HP call the theorem 'a mathematical version' of the Thirdness claim, so 054's 'non-reducibility of Thirdness' goes beyond the result. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] Specific to PAL; accurate summary of Burch if the source says so. |
| TR-S1-052 | G6 t6 | argument_step | Premise 1 (valency): PAL graph operations conserve valency, so compound relations inherit connectivity from their atomic constituents. | Burch (1991) | corrected | → The valency rule μ+ν−2λ is Herzberger's, now matched in two scans. PAL breaks it through juxtaposition (Koshkin 2022 §3), which is why Burch restricts juxtaposition. Burch 1992's abstract shows he reasons about 'valental' properties, but whether he argues from valency conservation is unread. [herzberger1981theorem] Valency conservation is more Herzberger's framing; verify Burch actually argues it this way. |
| TR-S1-053 | G6 t6 | argument_step | Premise 2 (branching): a monadic-plus-dyadic basis cannot form a 3-star node (teridentity) without an explicit ternary junction. | Burch (1991) | corrected | → The formal content is HCP 2006 Lemma 1: without id3, no more than two places of a term built from unary and binary relations are connected. Theorem 2 follows: id3 is not generated. '3-star node' is not source terminology. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] '3-star node' terminology looks like Gemini's; check source wording. |
| TR-S1-054 | G6 t6 | argument_step | Conclusion: genuine triadic relations cannot be generated from unary and binary relations, confirming the non-reducibility of Thirdness in closed algebraic systems. | Burch (1991) | corrected | → The negative clause is a theorem only in PAL without teridentity. PAL here means product, join of two places, complement and permutation, with id3 as the only built-in constant. For any set A with \|A\|≥2, the relations generated from all unary and binary relations do not include id3 (Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2006, Thm 2; Hereth & Pöschel 2011). Burch 1991 proved it only under his juxtaposition restriction. The clause fails under pp/relational-algebra closure, where =3 = ∆(ζ(= × =)) (Dau & Hereth Correia 2006, p.108), and under pairing or domain extension. Peirce argued the clause; he did not prove it ('proves' in 031 is wrong). HP call the theorem 'a mathematical version' of the Thirdness claim, so 054's 'non-reducibility of Thirdness' goes beyond the result. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] 'non-reducibility of Thirdness' goes beyond Burch's algebraic result (teridentity), a category-level inference. |
| TR-S1-055 | G6 t6 | logical | Premise 2: teridentity id3 = {(a,a,a)} intrinsically requires connectivity degree 3 among its coordinates. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2006) | corrected | → HCP 2006 proves two facts: all three places of id3 are essential (Lemma 3(ii), E(id3)=3), and terms without id3 over unary and binary relations have t-connected sets of at most 2 places (Lemma 1). 'Intrinsically requires connectivity degree 3' paraphrases that pair. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] 'intrinsically requires connectivity degree 3' is a paraphrase; verify against the paper. |
| TR-S1-056 | G2 t1 | rhetorical | Peircean teridentity is treated as a baseline theory, and the plan proposes parallels between it and UX design ethics. |  | out_of_scope |  |
| TR-S1-057 | G2 t2 | citation | The response says a previous synthesis, built on Peirce's Reduction Thesis, Quine's dyadic reduction and IIT, established the irreducibility result. | Charles Sanders Peirce, Reduction Thesis | out_of_scope | Self-citation of AI output; do not treat as evidence. |
| TR-S1-058 | G2 t2 | logical | Teridentity is a genuine triadic relation, and it cannot be generated from purely pairwise, dyadic connections (Gemini marker [1]). |  | corrected | → The teridentity half holds only in PAL without teridentity (HCP 2006 Thm 2), not under pp closure. The synergy half is an information-theory claim with no support in the PAL literature (see the iitpid cluster, williams2010decomposition). Do not join them under one citation. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] Lab result is split, not a clean yes: one-input wirings never give joint determination (cause side ≤2) but do give effect-side three-term facts at branch points. Do not cite as lab-supported. |
| TR-S1-059 | G2 t2 | definition | Peirce's side of the reduction question is described as his 'phenomenological trichotomy', meaning his categories. |  | corrected | → The 'phenomenological trichotomy' gloss on the categories is the Peirce cluster's call. On the Quine side, Quine 1954 never engages Peirce or the categories, so any 'historical conflict' is later framing. Quine's only engagement with the reduction clause was his 1935 review of the Collected Papers (Koshkin n. 1; unread). Reasonable gloss; 'historical conflict' with Quine is framing (Quine did not engage the categories). |
| TR-S1-060 | G2 t2 | logical | Teridentity is a branching node, and it is mathematically necessary for synchronizing variables across a complex system (Gemini marker [1]). |  | unverifiable | → In logic, what is sourced is narrower: in PAL, teridentity is needed to identify one variable across three or more places, e.g. for conjunction on shared variables (HCP 2004 Fig. 4). 'Synchronize variables across a complex system' has no source in the PAL literature. Drop it. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] If 'synchronize variables across a system' means integration/Φ>0, the lab contradicts it: pure copy cycles with no branch point are Φ=2.0 wholes, and every branched (teridentity-bearing) one-input wiring is Φ=0. Vague phrasing; the talk should drop it. [Refuter overturned 'lab_contradicted': Source context (G2 l.63) makes this a claim about first-order logic and relational clone theory: teridentity is needed to identify variables in Peirce/Quine/Burch algebra. The lab's dynamical results do not decide that. The contradiction works only if 'synchronize variables' is read as Φ>0. Read literally in the lab's rendering, synchronizing (B'=A, C'=A) happens at branch points, which fits the claim rather than refuting it. The evidence strings are verbatim but do not establish a contradiction.] |
| TR-S1-061 | G4 t1 | definition | Peircean Thirdness is glossed as genuine mediation, representation, and lawful integration. |  | corrected | → Mediation and representation are Peirce's glosses of Thirdness (CP 1.337, 1.339, 1.356; CP 3.422: "thirdness, or mediation"), and so is law (CP 1.26; CP 8.331: "possessor according to Law"). "Lawful integration" is not his phrase and must not be equated with IIT integration or Phi. [peirce1931papers] XAI context; 'lawful integration' not to be equated with Φ. |

In CP 2.274 the sign's three members are bound "in a way that does not consist in any complexus of dyadic relations." "Meaning generation" is the source's paraphrase. [peirce1931papers] Lab H4 is about a Boolean rendering and is not evidence for Peirce's text. |
| TR-S1-063 | G4 t4 | definition | Maps representamen to Firstness, object to Secondness, interpretant to Thirdness. |  | verified | → In outline, verified on the printed page. "Lawful integration" is not his phrase and must not be equated with IIT integration or Phi. [peirce1931papers] Same as row 045. |

| TR-S1-067 | P1 | historical | The irreducibility thesis originates in Peirce's 1897 logic of relatives. |  | corrected | → Both clauses first appear in the 1870 memoir, p. 374 (CP 3.144), re-read on the page image. The volume is dated 1873, and the memoir was communicated on 26 January 1870. The valency argument follows in 1887-88 ('A Guess at the Riddle', CP 1.363; dated 1887-88 in W6, c. 1890 in CP), and the reply to Kempe in 1892 (CP 3.423-424). The 1897 Monist article (p. 183) restates the thesis with the (μ+ν−2λ) formula. TR-S1-068 has no card yet and should get peirce1870description. [peirce1870description] Wrong as an origin date: the negative clause first appears in 1870 (lab literature report cites CP 3.144, but confirm in primary). 1897 is a later full statement. |

The volume is dated 1873, and the memoir was communicated on 26 January 1870. The valency argument follows in 1887-88 ('A Guess at the Riddle', CP 1.363; dated 1887-88 in W6, c. 1890 in CP), and the reply to Kempe in 1892 (CP 3.423-424). The 1897 Monist article (p. 183) restates the thesis with the (μ+ν−2λ) formula. TR-S1-068 has no card yet and should get peirce1870description. No PAL source makes the information-theory half. Present it as the talk's analogy. | TR-S1-071 | P1 | logical | Genuine triadic relations cannot be generated from monadic properties or dyadic channels over a closed universe. |  | corrected | → Both classical reductions change the domain. Löwenheim moves to pairs of old elements, which is n² for n individuals. Quine closes the universe under {x, y}. Holding the universe fixed is not enough for irreducibility. Koshkin (arXiv 2406.14094, Cor. 1) shows that 'Any n-ary with n ≥ 3 on an infinite domain reduces to a projoin of n binaries'. Irreducibility needs restricted operations as well: PAL without teridentity, or Herzberger's no-branching valency rule. Koshkin also shows it on small finite domains. 'The proofs confirm it' holds only inside those operation sets. 'Artificial' is Gemini's word. [lowenheim1915moglichkeiten] 'closed universe' caveat is correct in spirit: the reduction via pairing enlarges the domain. |
| TR-S1-072 | P1 | definition | Firstness corresponds to the monad: unary selection and uncoupled potentiality. |  | corrected | → The Peircean half is supported. Firstness is quality and possibility (CP 1.25), the pure monad is "a pure nature, or quality" (CP 1.303), and the first is "present and immediate" (CP 1.357). The pairing with Shannon or marginal entropy H(X), "baseline noise" and "uncoupled uncertainty" has no Peircean locus; it is the sources' analogy. "Ground" was not checked. | TR-S1-073 | P1 | definition | Secondness corresponds to the dyad: pairwise point-to-point connections. |  | corrected | → Peirce ties the dyad to Secondness himself (CP 8.330). Secondness as resistance and reaction is in CP 1.324, pairs and causation in CP 1.325, and "suffers and yet resists" in CP 1.358. "Relate" is his vocabulary (CP 8.330: "The Second, or Relate"). Three things are the sources' framing, not Peirce's: "formalize pairwise coupling", the alignment with equality and composition operations, and Shannon mutual information. [peirce1931papers] 'Shannon mutual information' is Gemini's analogy. |
| TR-S1-074 | P1 | definition | Thirdness is the teridentity relation =_3 = {(x,x,x) \| x in X}. |  | refuted | → The PAL authors call the teridentity theorem 'a mathematical version' of the Thirdness claim. They do not identify Thirdness with =3. Burch's criterion, known only from Koshkin's transcription of p.117, locates Thirdness in operations that presuppose a non-degenerate triad. Say 'teridentity is the PAL witness of genuine triadicity', not 'Thirdness is =3'. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] Category/relation conflation (see row 019); definition of =_3 itself is correct. |

[hereth2011logic] 'PAL proves' should cite Burch; 'synthesized Thirdness' is framing. |

| TR-S2-001 | D4 | citation | Cites Simmel (coalitions, tertius gaudens). | Simmel | corrected | → Unchanged verdict. Every slide locus in all three editions is now matched on the page images. Small's 1902 English uses 'coalition' and also 'dyad' and 'triad' (I: 44, II: 182). What Simmel lacks is Caplow's theory of which pair forms. [simmel1950sociology; simmel1902number2; simmel1908soziologie] The lab has its own computational tests (probes #369–#373 incl. #373 H5 tertius gaudens CONFIRMED, #371 H3 majority REFUTED, org_frontier/thinkers/simmel/), but those cannot vouch for what Simmel wrote. Verified Simmel cards exist at submissions/slacker_thirds/library/cards/simmel1908soziologie.md and simmel1950sociology.md. Note that 'coalitions' is Caplow's term, not Simmel's. |
| TR-S2-002 | D4 | citation | Cites Caplow (coalitions in the triad). | Caplow | unverifiable | → Both works exist as cited. Crossref confirms Caplow, 'A Theory of Coalitions in the Triad', ASR 21(4), Aug. 1956, from p. 489, DOI 10.2307/2088718. The end page 493 rests only on the lab bib and secondary citations. Open Library confirms Two Against One (Prentice-Hall, 1968). Neither text was read, since the article is closed access with no OA copy. The lab's description in paper.md l.87 ('six triad types', coalitions derived from power distributions) is unverified. Card keys follow the firstnoun rule (caplow1956theory, caplow1968coalitions); the lab bib keys are caplow1956coalitions and caplow1968two. [caplow1956theory; caplow1968coalitions] No Caplow card in slacker_thirds/library/cards; acquire and card the 1956 ASR article before quoting. [metadata only: the work exists as cited; its text was not read] |

## S3 — Quine and Löwenheim (108)

| id | source | kind | claim | cited work | status | note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TR-S3-001 | D1 | historical | In 1954 Quine presented an apparent decisive counterproof to Peirce's thesis. | Quine 1954, Reduction to a Dyadic Predicate | refuted | → Peirce is not named on p. 180 or in either footnote (pp. 181-182 unread). Quine's n. 1 calls the result 'reminiscent of, but distinct from' Kalmár's and Church-Craig-Quine's, not Peirce's. Koshkin 2022 (n. 1), not 2024, says Quine 'made no mention of Peirce, PRT or what his 1954 reduction to dyads meant for it then, or later', and that 'His silence is usually interpreted as a courteous submission that PRT is false, but he never did say so himself.' The anti-Peirce reading comes from the reception. Koshkin p. 1 counts Löwenheim 1915 and Quine 1954 among the 'counterclaims', and Burch (SEP §14) says the thesis was 'doubted by many, especially after the publication of a proof by Willard Van Orman Quine'. Koshkin's named example of someone who pressed the pairing construction 'as an objection to PRT' is Christopherson & Johnstone, TCSPS 17 (1981): 241-246 (christopherson1981triadicity). Peirce died in 1914, so no 1954 debate took place. Replace every 'Koshkin 2022 (arXiv 2406.14058)' in the locus with 'Koshkin 2022'. [quine1954reduction] Gemini frames Quine 1954 as a counterproof to Peirce. The paper may not mention Peirce; the Peirce–Quine opposition comes from later readers. Löwenheim (1915, Math. Ann. 76) gave the reduction to binary relations earlier and appears in none of the sources. The lab's own gloss (peirce/paper.md:39) is only 'Löwenheim and Quine showed that set-theoretic pairing reduces any relation to dyads'. |
| TR-S3-002 | D1 | logical | Quine's 1954 translation shows every polyadic formal system reduces to a theory with a single dyadic predicate. | Quine 1954 | corrected | → Scope: any interpreted theory written in first-order quantification theory. It translates into a theory Θ′ whose single predicate letter is dyadic. The letter is 'F', never 'J'. F is not set membership. The proof assumes 'a fragment of set theory' and a universe for Θ′ closed under unordered pairing. TR-S3-074 is nearly Quine's wording. TR-S3-015 and TR-S3-049 ('membership as sole primitive') are wrong. The 'three operations' of TR-S3-007 are D1's reconstruction. Quine's own steps are Kuratowski pairs nested to the right, a universe closed under {x, y}, and each atomic sentence coded as nw1;w1;w1;w2;...;w_dn, with F reflexive on that code. [quine1954reduction] Check how Quine words his scope (e.g. 'theory formulated in quantification theory') and whether he says 'every polyadic formal system'. D1's 'extensional translation' is Gemini's label. |
| TR-S3-003 | D1 | argument_step | Quine's translation relies on set-theoretic pairing functions that expand the ontology into an infinite universe of sets. |  | corrected | → Quine does enlarge the universe. Θ′ ranges over Θ's objects plus {x, y} for every x and y in Θ′, so the universe is closed under unordered pairing. He assumes only a set-theory fragment that guarantees {x, y} and that x differs from {x, y} and {{x}}. The paper mentions no ∅, no 'inductive set universe', and never uses the word 'infinite'. On a standard well-founded reading the closure is infinite, but Quine does not say so. He adds that Θ's universe may already contain the pairs, in which case nothing is added. 'Solely' and 'only' overstate. TR-S3-029's 'meta-theoretic ontology' is wrong: the pairs belong to Θ′'s object-level universe. TR-S3-018 and TR-S3-102 are correct: the translation does not stay within Θ's universe. For TR-S3-017, attribute the reconciliation to Burch (2021, SEP, quoted in Koshkin 2024 p. 4): 'both Peirce and Quine were correct: the issue entirely depends on exactly what constructive resources are to be allowed'. [quine1954reduction] Unverified that Quine's construction uses an infinite universe of sets; over a finite domain, coding tuples needs only finitely many new objects. Check before saying 'infinite'. |
| TR-S3-004 | D1 | argument_step | Quine's translation covertly smuggles in the teridentity relation =3 = {(x,x,x)}. |  | corrected | → A published source makes the substance of this claim: Koshkin 2022, not Quine's text and not Burch. Koshkin p. 2: 'the pairing construction, as formalized in set theory, relies on the very FOL devices that are explicated as using triads.' On p. 7 he writes the pairing step as a triple junction ∃t[R(1)(t,x1) ∧ R(2)(t,x2) ∧ R̂(t,x3)] that 'conceals triadicity'. PAL simulates a triple junction with the teridentity I3 (p. 5, Fig. 2a), so in Koshkin's explication the pairing reduction uses a teridentity. Four corrections. (a) Quine has no triadic Pair(x,y,p) primitive, and Koshkin's device is an existential pair-element plus three dyads. (b) The mechanism is 'syntactic absorption' of object-level variable identification and branch points, not 'meta-syntax' or 'meta-linguistic coordinate binding'. (c) The argument is an EG/FOL explication, not a 'universal-algebraic' or clone-theoretic proof, and 'join-irreducible' is not Koshkin's term (TR-S3-081, -104). (d) Koshkin speaks of 'the set-theoretic pairing construction' and 'pairing reductions a la Löwenheim and Quine' (p. 4), not of Quine's formula (1). Christopherson & Johnstone 1981 argued on the other side: per Koshkin they pressed pairing against PRT. They do not support this claim. [koshkin2022reduction] 'Covertly smuggling in' is Gemini's gloss; no source quote supports it. PAL results show teridentity is needed within PAL's operations; that is not the same as a finding about Quine's construction. |
| TR-S3-005 | D1 | argument_step | Teridentity is smuggled in via meta-linguistic coordinate binding and the triadic pairing relation Pair(x, y, p). |  | corrected | → A published source makes the substance of this claim: Koshkin 2022, not Quine's text and not Burch. Koshkin p. 2: 'the pairing construction, as formalized in set theory, relies on the very FOL devices that are explicated as using triads.' On p. 7 he writes the pairing step as a triple junction ∃t[R(1)(t,x1) ∧ R(2)(t,x2) ∧ R̂(t,x3)] that 'conceals triadicity'. PAL simulates a triple junction with the teridentity I3 (p. 5, Fig. 2a), so in Koshkin's explication the pairing reduction uses a teridentity. Four corrections. (a) Quine has no triadic Pair(x,y,p) primitive, and Koshkin's device is an existential pair-element plus three dyads. (b) The mechanism is 'syntactic absorption' of object-level variable identification and branch points, not 'meta-syntax' or 'meta-linguistic coordinate binding'. (c) The argument is an EG/FOL explication, not a 'universal-algebraic' or clone-theoretic proof, and 'join-irreducible' is not Koshkin's term (TR-S3-081, -104). (d) Koshkin speaks of 'the set-theoretic pairing construction' and 'pairing reductions a la Löwenheim and Quine' (p. 4), not of Quine's formula (1). Christopherson & Johnstone 1981 argued on the other side: per Koshkin they pressed pairing against PRT. They do not support this claim. [koshkin2022reduction] 'Meta-linguistic coordinate binding' has no known source; it is probably Gemini's phrase. |
| TR-S3-006 | D1 | citation | Quine's paper 'Reduction to a Dyadic Predicate' advanced a universal syntactic translation. | Quine 1954, Reduction to a Dyadic Predicate | verified | → Quine, W. V. 'Reduction to a dyadic predicate.' The Journal of Symbolic Logic 19(3): 180-182 (September 1954), doi 10.2307/2268616. Crossref, Cambridge Core and wvquine.org agree, including issue 3. Reprinted in Selected Logic Papers. TR-S3-035's companion, Cobham, 'Reduction to a symmetric predicate', is JSL 21(1): 56-59 (1956), doi 10.2307/2268487. TR-S3-036 is a bare volume link, not a citation. 'Universal syntactic translation' (TR-S3-006) is Gemini's phrase. [quine1954reduction] 'Universal syntactic translation' is Gemini's description, not Quine's wording. |
| TR-S3-007 | D1 | logical | Any theory with relation symbols of arbitrary arity can be rearticulated with a single dyadic predicate J, via three operations. |  | corrected | → Scope: any interpreted theory written in first-order quantification theory. It translates into a theory Θ′ whose single predicate letter is dyadic. The letter is 'F', never 'J'. F is not set membership. The proof assumes 'a fragment of set theory' and a universe for Θ′ closed under unordered pairing. TR-S3-074 is nearly Quine's wording. TR-S3-015 and TR-S3-049 ('membership as sole primitive') are wrong. The 'three operations' of TR-S3-007 are D1's reconstruction. Quine's own steps are Kuratowski pairs nested to the right, a universe closed under {x, y}, and each atomic sentence coded as nw1;w1;w1;w2;...;w_dn, with F reflexive on that code. [quine1954reduction] The three-step decomposition (tupling, domain inflation, reification) is D1's reconstruction. |
| TR-S3-008 | D1 | definition | Step 1: a polyadic relation becomes a statement about one n-tuple defined recursively by nested ordered pairs <x1,<x2,...>>. |  | verified | → Right-nested, as D1 says: x;(y;z). The atomic code also carries the predicate's index: x = nw1;w1;w1;w2;w3;...;w_dn, where nw is Quine's name for w;...;w with n terms. TR-S3-077 is G6's recap and should not be quoted as Quine's wording. [quine1954reduction] Check whether Quine nests to the right (<x1,<x2,...>>) or the left. |
| TR-S3-009 | D1 | historical | Quine uses the Kuratowski pair <u,v> = {{u},{u,v}}. |  | corrected | → Quine names Kuratowski's pair {{x}, {x, y}}. 'Wiener-Kuratowski' (TR-S3-031, TR-S3-099, TR-S3-100) conflates two definitions. Quine does not use Wiener's. The text read contains no claim 'to avoid ternary primitives' (TR-S3-022). TR-S3-051 is right in substance: Θ′'s universe is closed under unordered pairs {x, y}, and so under Kuratowski pairs. [quine1954reduction] D1 hedges between Kuratowski and Wiener (TR-S3-010), so it does not know which one Quine used. |
| TR-S3-010 | D1 | historical | Alternatively Quine uses Wiener's earlier pair <u,v> = {{{u},∅},{{v}}}. |  | refuted | → Quine uses Kuratowski's pair, not Wiener's {{{u},∅},{{v}}}. [quine1954reduction] Wiener's pair is {{{a},∅},{{b}}} and predates Kuratowski's (1921). It is unverified that Quine 1954 uses either. |
| TR-S3-011 | D1 | definition | Step 2 (domain inflation): the domain X is enlarged to an inductive set universe X* closed under pairing, with ∅ and an infinite set hierarchy. |  | corrected | → Quine does enlarge the universe. Θ′ ranges over Θ's objects plus {x, y} for every x and y in Θ′, so the universe is closed under unordered pairing. He assumes only a set-theory fragment that guarantees {x, y} and that x differs from {x, y} and {{x}}. The paper mentions no ∅, no 'inductive set universe', and never uses the word 'infinite'. On a standard well-founded reading the closure is infinite, but Quine does not say so. He adds that Θ's universe may already contain the pairs, in which case nothing is added. 'Solely' and 'only' overstate. TR-S3-029's 'meta-theoretic ontology' is wrong: the pairs belong to Θ′'s object-level universe. TR-S3-018 and TR-S3-102 are correct: the translation does not stay within Θ's universe. For TR-S3-017, attribute the reconciliation to Burch (2021, SEP, quoted in Koshkin 2024 p. 4): 'both Peirce and Quine were correct: the issue entirely depends on exactly what constructive resources are to be allowed'. [quine1954reduction] The 'inductive set universe with ∅ and an infinite hierarchy' is D1's detail. Check before using it; see the note on TR-S3-003 about whether infinity is needed. |
| TR-S3-012 | D1 | definition | Step 3: Quine assigns each primitive predicate an individual entity (a set or code) in X*. |  | corrected | → Quine assigns no entity or code to a predicate. The index n of F_n enters each atomic sentence's code as the prefix nw1, which is w1;...;w1 with n terms: x = nw1;w1;w1;w2;...;w_dn. [quine1954reduction] |
| TR-S3-013 | D1 | logical | Each atomic formula R_i(x1..xn) is translated as J(<x1..xn>, c_Ri): the tuple stands in binary relation J to the reified extension. |  | refuted | → Neither J(<x1..xn>, c_R) nor J(<x1,x2>, y) is Quine's. F has two disjuncts. Either x is a pair-set containing y, or x = y and x codes a true atomic sentence F_n w1...w_dn. So F_n w1...w_dn holds exactly when F relates the code nw1;w1;w1;w2;...;w_dn to itself. The first disjunct cannot fire when x = y, because Quine assumes x ≠ {x, z}. His explicit definitions inside Θ′ are on pp. 181-182, which remain unread. [quine1954reduction] The sources disagree on the schema: D1 writes J(<x1..xn>, c_R), with the predicate reified; D1 elsewhere and P1 write R(x1,x2,y) ⇒ J(<x1,x2>, y) (TR-S3-026, TR-S3-107). Reproduce Quine's actual clause. |
| TR-S3-014 | D1 | definition | The single dyadic relation J is effectively set membership. |  | corrected | → F is not set membership. Only its first disjunct is membership-like: (∃z)(x = {y, z}), so y belongs to the pair-set x. The second disjunct makes F reflexive on codes of true atomic sentences. There is no 'J'. [quine1954reduction] 'Effectively set membership' is D1's hedge. |
| TR-S3-015 | D1 | historical | Quine reasoned that any polyadic first-order theory can be modeled with dyadic membership as sole non-logical primitive. |  | corrected | → Scope: any interpreted theory written in first-order quantification theory. It translates into a theory Θ′ whose single predicate letter is dyadic. The letter is 'F', never 'J'. F is not set membership. The proof assumes 'a fragment of set theory' and a universe for Θ′ closed under unordered pairing. TR-S3-074 is nearly Quine's wording. TR-S3-015 and TR-S3-049 ('membership as sole primitive') are wrong. The 'three operations' of TR-S3-007 are D1's reconstruction. Quine's own steps are Kuratowski pairs nested to the right, a universe closed under {x, y}, and each atomic sentence coded as nw1;w1;w1;w2;...;w_dn, with F reflexive on that code. [quine1954reduction] This row attributes reasoning to Quine; quote him or drop the attribution. |
| TR-S3-016 | D1 | historical | Quine concluded that relations of arity three or more have no fundamental logical status. |  | unverifiable | → The claim does not appear on p. 180 or in the notes. The paper is a translatability result, and pp. 181-182 are unread. Do not attribute this conclusion to Quine. [quine1954reduction] This looks like Gemini's extrapolation. A three-page technical note is unlikely to state such a philosophical conclusion. Do not use it without a quotation. |
| TR-S3-017 | D1 | argument_step | The Quine-Burch contradiction is resolved by Quine's reliance on model expansion. |  | corrected | → Quine does enlarge the universe. Θ′ ranges over Θ's objects plus {x, y} for every x and y in Θ′, so the universe is closed under unordered pairing. He assumes only a set-theory fragment that guarantees {x, y} and that x differs from {x, y} and {{x}}. The paper mentions no ∅, no 'inductive set universe', and never uses the word 'infinite'. On a standard well-founded reading the closure is infinite, but Quine does not say so. He adds that Θ's universe may already contain the pairs, in which case nothing is added. 'Solely' and 'only' overstate. TR-S3-029's 'meta-theoretic ontology' is wrong: the pairs belong to Θ′'s object-level universe. TR-S3-018 and TR-S3-102 are correct: the translation does not stay within Θ's universe. For TR-S3-017, attribute the reconciliation to Burch (2021, SEP, quoted in Koshkin 2024 p. 4): 'both Peirce and Quine were correct: the issue entirely depends on exactly what constructive resources are to be allowed'. [quine1954reduction] The reconciliation (Quine changes the universe; PAL works within a fixed universe) is plausible, but you need a logician's statement of it. Frame it as the logicians' reply, not as 'model expansion' in Gemini's words. |
| TR-S3-018 | D1 | argument_step | Quine's reduction does not build polyadic relations from dyadic ones within the original universe A. |  | corrected | → Quine does enlarge the universe. Θ′ ranges over Θ's objects plus {x, y} for every x and y in Θ′, so the universe is closed under unordered pairing. He assumes only a set-theory fragment that guarantees {x, y} and that x differs from {x, y} and {{x}}. The paper mentions no ∅, no 'inductive set universe', and never uses the word 'infinite'. On a standard well-founded reading the closure is infinite, but Quine does not say so. He adds that Θ's universe may already contain the pairs, in which case nothing is added. 'Solely' and 'only' overstate. TR-S3-029's 'meta-theoretic ontology' is wrong: the pairs belong to Θ′'s object-level universe. TR-S3-018 and TR-S3-102 are correct: the translation does not stay within Θ's universe. For TR-S3-017, attribute the reconciliation to Burch (2021, SEP, quoted in Koshkin 2024 p. 4): 'both Peirce and Quine were correct: the issue entirely depends on exactly what constructive resources are to be allowed'. [quine1954reduction] |
| TR-S3-019 | D1 | argument_step | It simulates relations over A by embedding them in an infinite set-theoretic universe X* ⊃ A. |  | corrected | → Quine does enlarge the universe. Θ′ ranges over Θ's objects plus {x, y} for every x and y in Θ′, so the universe is closed under unordered pairing. He assumes only a set-theory fragment that guarantees {x, y} and that x differs from {x, y} and {{x}}. The paper mentions no ∅, no 'inductive set universe', and never uses the word 'infinite'. On a standard well-founded reading the closure is infinite, but Quine does not say so. He adds that Θ's universe may already contain the pairs, in which case nothing is added. 'Solely' and 'only' overstate. TR-S3-029's 'meta-theoretic ontology' is wrong: the pairs belong to Θ′'s object-level universe. TR-S3-018 and TR-S3-102 are correct: the translation does not stay within Θ's universe. For TR-S3-017, attribute the reconciliation to Burch (2021, SEP, quoted in Koshkin 2024 p. 4): 'both Peirce and Quine were correct: the issue entirely depends on exactly what constructive resources are to be allowed'. [quine1954reduction] Whether the enlarged universe is infinite is unverified; see TR-S3-003. |
| TR-S3-020 | D1 | argument_step | Quine's encoding requires a pairing function Pair(x1, x2, p) iff p = <x1, x2>. |  | corrected | → Quine has no Pair(x1, x2, p) predicate. Θ′'s only primitive is dyadic F. Identity and 'x = {y, z}' are defined within Θ′ from F. The pairing sets are assumed set-theoretically for the interpretation. The 'Quine's Fallacy' heading is Gemini's rhetoric. (The bracketed formulas are inline images in the transcription.) [quine1954reduction] Pair(x1,x2,p) is Gemini's notation; D1 then says Quine defines the pair away (TR-S3-022). The two rows sit in tension. |
| TR-S3-021 | D1 | logical | Pairing is an irreducible triadic relation connecting first coordinate, second coordinate, and the pair. |  | corrected | → HP 2011 uses the graph of a pairing bijection µ: A×A→A, a ternary relation, to reduce arity on infinite domains. So in PAL's bookkeeping pairing counts as a triad. No source read proves the pairing graph irreducible. Under pp/FOL with ∈, pairing is definable from a dyadic predicate plus variable identification, which is where the triad hides (Dau & HC p.107-108). [hereth2011logic] This is a formal claim. 'Irreducible' is relative to an operation set: in PAL it may hold, but in first-order logic with ∈, pairing is definable from a dyadic predicate. State which algebra is meant. |
| TR-S3-022 | D1 | historical | Quine claims to avoid ternary primitives by defining p = {{x1},{x1,x2}}. |  | corrected | → Quine names Kuratowski's pair {{x}, {x, y}}. 'Wiener-Kuratowski' (TR-S3-031, TR-S3-099, TR-S3-100) conflates two definitions. Quine does not use Wiener's. The text read contains no claim 'to avoid ternary primitives' (TR-S3-022). TR-S3-051 is right in substance: Θ′'s universe is closed under unordered pairs {x, y}, and so under Kuratowski pairs. [quine1954reduction] Unverified that Quine says he 'claims to avoid ternary primitives'. |
| TR-S3-023 | D1 | argument_step | That definition needs axiomatic set theory, shifting pairing's triadic mechanism into the meta-logic of class formation and quantification. |  | corrected | → The core charge, that the pairing reduction hides triadic binding, has a published source: Koshkin 2022, p. 2 ('the pairing construction, as formalized in set theory, relies on the very FOL devices that are explicated as using triads') and p. 7 (pairing as a triple junction that 'conceals triadicity'). Corrections: (1) Nothing moves into meta-syntax or 'meta-logic'. Quine defines pairs and identity inside Θ′, and Koshkin puts the hidden triad in object-level FOL variable identification and branch points ('syntactic absorption'). (2) Quine assumes 'a fragment of set theory', not 'the full apparatus of axiomatic set theory' (TR-S3-023). (3) The theorem does not 'fail' (TR-S3-093). Koshkin calls Quine 'correct in a technical algebraic sense' and wrong only 'in a deeper conceptual sense' (p. 4). (4) No Pair(x,y,p) predicate exists in Quine (TR-S3-045). 'Expanding ontology into sets' (TR-S3-024) is right (p. 180). TR-S3-060 keeps its earlier 'corrected' verdict under koshkin2022reduction. Replace 'Burch 2021 SEP, via Koshkin 2024 p. 4' with 'Burch SEP 2021 §14 (read directly); Koshkin 2022 p. 4'. [quine1954reduction] 'Full apparatus of axiomatic set theory' may overstate the case; the construction may need only a pairing structure. Check it. |
| TR-S3-024 | D1 | argument_step | Quine eliminated polyadic predicates only by smuggling ternary branching into variable bindings and expanding ontology into sets. |  | corrected | → The core charge, that the pairing reduction hides triadic binding, has a published source: Koshkin 2022, p. 2 ('the pairing construction, as formalized in set theory, relies on the very FOL devices that are explicated as using triads') and p. 7 (pairing as a triple junction that 'conceals triadicity'). Corrections: (1) Nothing moves into meta-syntax or 'meta-logic'. Quine defines pairs and identity inside Θ′, and Koshkin puts the hidden triad in object-level FOL variable identification and branch points ('syntactic absorption'). (2) Quine assumes 'a fragment of set theory', not 'the full apparatus of axiomatic set theory' (TR-S3-023). (3) The theorem does not 'fail' (TR-S3-093). Koshkin calls Quine 'correct in a technical algebraic sense' and wrong only 'in a deeper conceptual sense' (p. 4). (4) No Pair(x,y,p) predicate exists in Quine (TR-S3-045). 'Expanding ontology into sets' (TR-S3-024) is right (p. 180). TR-S3-060 keeps its earlier 'corrected' verdict under koshkin2022reduction. Replace 'Burch 2021 SEP, via Koshkin 2024 p. 4' with 'Burch SEP 2021 §14 (read directly); Koshkin 2022 p. 4'. [quine1954reduction] This is a Gemini thesis with no quoted source. The PAL literature says the thesis holds when the operations exclude branching (Herzberger's valency rule) or treat it as teridentity (Burch); 'smuggling' is Gemini's own charge against Quine. |
| TR-S3-025 | D1 | citation | Pillar 1 lists Quine 1954, J. Symb. Log. 19(3): 180-182. | Quine, W.V.O. (1954) Reduction to a Dyadic Predicate, J. Symb. Log. 19(3):180-182 | verified | → Quine, W. V. 'Reduction to a dyadic predicate.' The Journal of Symbolic Logic 19(3): 180-182 (September 1954), doi 10.2307/2268616. Crossref, Cambridge Core and wvquine.org agree, including issue 3. Reprinted in Selected Logic Papers. TR-S3-035's companion, Cobham, 'Reduction to a symmetric predicate', is JSL 21(1): 56-59 (1956), doi 10.2307/2268487. [quine1954reduction] The volume and page numbers agree across D1, G6 and P1, but confirm the issue number against the journal record. |
| TR-S3-026 | D1 | argument_step | Quine's reduction is glossed as representing any polyadic dependency via intermediate pairs: R(x1,x2,y) ⇒ J(<x1,x2>, y). |  | refuted | → Neither J(<x1..xn>, c_R) nor J(<x1,x2>, y) is Quine's. So F_n w1...w_dn holds exactly when F relates the code nw1;w1;w1;w2;...;w_dn to itself. The first disjunct cannot fire when x = y, because Quine assumes x ≠ {x, z}. His explicit definitions inside Θ′ are on pp. 181-182, which remain unread. [quine1954reduction] J(<x1,x2>, y) conflicts with D1's own J(<x1..xn>, c_R) (TR-S3-013). At most one of them is Quine's. |
| TR-S3-027 | D1 | argument_step | Quine's reduction succeeds only by moving triadic binding into an infinite set universe. |  | corrected | → Quine does enlarge the universe. Θ′ ranges over Θ's objects plus {x, y} for every x and y in Θ′, so the universe is closed under unordered pairing. He assumes only a set-theory fragment that guarantees {x, y} and that x differs from {x, y} and {{x}}. The paper mentions no ∅, no 'inductive set universe', and never uses the word 'infinite'. On a standard well-founded reading the closure is infinite, but Quine does not say so. He adds that Θ's universe may already contain the pairs, in which case nothing is added. 'Solely' and 'only' overstate. TR-S3-029's 'meta-theoretic ontology' is wrong: the pairs belong to Θ′'s object-level universe. TR-S3-018 and TR-S3-102 are correct: the translation does not stay within Θ's universe. [quine1954reduction] 'Triadic binding' is Gemini's term. |
| TR-S3-028 | D1 | historical | Abstract: Quine (1954) challenged the thesis, reducing polyadic theories to one dyadic predicate via ordered pairs. | Quine 1954 | refuted | → Peirce is not named on p. 180 or in either footnote (pp. 181-182 unread). Quine's n. 1 calls the result 'reminiscent of, but distinct from' Kalmár's and Church-Craig-Quine's, not Peirce's. Koshkin 2022 (n. 1), not 2024, says Quine 'made no mention of Peirce, PRT or what his 1954 reduction to dyads meant for it then, or later', and that 'His silence is usually interpreted as a courteous submission that PRT is false, but he never did say so himself.' The anti-Peirce reading comes from the reception. Koshkin p. 1 counts Löwenheim 1915 and Quine 1954 among the 'counterclaims', and Burch (SEP §14) says the thesis was 'doubted by many, especially after the publication of a proof by Willard Van Orman Quine'. Koshkin's named example of someone who pressed the pairing construction 'as an objection to PRT' is Christopherson & Johnstone, TCSPS 17 (1981): 241-246 (christopherson1981triadicity). Peirce died in 1914, so no 1954 debate took place. Replace every 'Koshkin 2022 (arXiv 2406.14058)' in the locus with 'Koshkin 2022'. [quine1954reduction] 'Challenged this thesis' may misattribute intent; see TR-S3-001. |
| TR-S3-029 | D1 | argument_step | Quine's reduction succeeds only by externalizing triadic binding into an infinite meta-theoretic ontology. |  | corrected | → Quine does enlarge the universe. Θ′ ranges over Θ's objects plus {x, y} for every x and y in Θ′, so the universe is closed under unordered pairing. He assumes only a set-theory fragment that guarantees {x, y} and that x differs from {x, y} and {{x}}. The paper mentions no ∅, no 'inductive set universe', and never uses the word 'infinite'. On a standard well-founded reading the closure is infinite, but Quine does not say so. He adds that Θ's universe may already contain the pairs, in which case nothing is added. 'Solely' and 'only' overstate. TR-S3-029's 'meta-theoretic ontology' is wrong: the pairs belong to Θ′'s object-level universe. TR-S3-018 and TR-S3-102 are correct: the translation does not stay within Θ's universe. For TR-S3-017, attribute the reconciliation to Burch (2021, SEP, quoted in Koshkin 2024 p. 4): 'both Peirce and Quine were correct: the issue entirely depends on exactly what constructive resources are to be allowed'. [quine1954reduction] 'Meta-theoretic ontology' confuses the object-level universe with the metatheory. The new objects are in the translated theory's domain, not in the metatheory. |
| TR-S3-030 | D1 | historical | Frames Quine's program as radical nominalism and dyadic reduction against Peirce's trichotomy. |  | refuted | → The 1954 construction quantifies over sets {x, y}, so it is not a nominalist construction. The paper also sets up no opposition to Peirce's categories. [quine1954reduction] Likely inaccurate: Quine's pairing construction quantifies over classes, and by the 1950s he accepted classes, so 'radical nominalism' sits badly with the 1954 reduction. |
| TR-S3-031 | D1 | historical | Quine 1954 relies on Wiener-Kuratowski pairs, infinite class hierarchies, predicate reification. | Quine 1954, Reduction to a Dyadic Predicate | corrected | → Quine names Kuratowski's pair {{x}, {x, y}}. 'Wiener-Kuratowski' (TR-S3-031, TR-S3-099, TR-S3-100) conflates two definitions. Quine does not use Wiener's. The text read contains no claim 'to avoid ternary primitives' (TR-S3-022). TR-S3-051 is right in substance: Θ′'s universe is closed under unordered pairs {x, y}, and so under Kuratowski pairs. [quine1954reduction] 'Wiener-Kuratowski' conflates two different pair definitions; see TR-S3-010 and TR-S3-100. |
| TR-S3-032 | D1 | rhetorical | Core paradox: if Quine's proof is sound, why do multivariable dependencies resist dyadic reduction? |  | out_of_scope | It presupposes that multivariable dependencies resist dyadic reduction. The lab's Peirce H1 found that one-input wirings do compose into adicity-3/4 facts (peirce/FINDINGS.md:20), so do not adopt this framing. |
| TR-S3-033 | D1 | argument_step | Quine's logic also presupposes teridentity inside the triadic pairing relation Pair(x1, x2, p). |  | corrected | → A published source makes the substance of this claim: Koshkin 2022, not Quine's text and not Burch. Koshkin p. 2: 'the pairing construction, as formalized in set theory, relies on the very FOL devices that are explicated as using triads.' On p. 7 he writes the pairing step as a triple junction ∃t[R(1)(t,x1) ∧ R(2)(t,x2) ∧ R̂(t,x3)] that 'conceals triadicity'. PAL simulates a triple junction with the teridentity I3 (p. 5, Fig. 2a), so in Koshkin's explication the pairing reduction uses a teridentity. Four corrections. (a) Quine has no triadic Pair(x,y,p) primitive, and Koshkin's device is an existential pair-element plus three dyads. (b) The mechanism is 'syntactic absorption' of object-level variable identification and branch points, not 'meta-syntax' or 'meta-linguistic coordinate binding'. (c) The argument is an EG/FOL explication, not a 'universal-algebraic' or clone-theoretic proof, and 'join-irreducible' is not Koshkin's term (TR-S3-081, -104). (d) Koshkin speaks of 'the set-theoretic pairing construction' and 'pairing reductions a la Löwenheim and Quine' (p. 4), not of Quine's formula (1). Christopherson & Johnstone 1981 argued on the other side: per Koshkin they pressed pairing against PRT. They do not support this claim. [koshkin2022reduction] This is Gemini's thesis. No source is quoted. |
| TR-S3-034 | D1 | argument_step | Quine's reduction works only by inflating ontology and shifting triadic binding into pairing's meta-logic. |  | corrected | → Quine does enlarge the universe. Θ′ ranges over Θ's objects plus {x, y} for every x and y in Θ′, so the universe is closed under unordered pairing. He assumes only a set-theory fragment that guarantees {x, y} and that x differs from {x, y} and {{x}}. The paper mentions no ∅, no 'inductive set universe', and never uses the word 'infinite'. On a standard well-founded reading the closure is infinite, but Quine does not say so. He adds that Θ's universe may already contain the pairs, in which case nothing is added. 'Solely' and 'only' overstate. TR-S3-029's 'meta-theoretic ontology' is wrong: the pairs belong to Θ′'s object-level universe. TR-S3-018 and TR-S3-102 are correct: the translation does not stay within Θ's universe. For TR-S3-017, attribute the reconciliation to Burch (2021, SEP, quoted in Koshkin 2024 p. 4): 'both Peirce and Quine were correct: the issue entirely depends on exactly what constructive resources are to be allowed'. [quine1954reduction] 'Works only by' is stronger than any source quoted. |
| TR-S3-035 | D1 | citation | Works-cited entry: 4. W. V. Quine, Reduction to a dyadic predicate, JSL 19 (1954) 180-182, with Cobham, Reduction to a symmetric predicate, JSL 21 (1956) | 4. W. V. Quine, Reduction to a dyadic predicate, JSL 19 (1954) 180-182, with Cobham, Reduction to a symmetric predicate, JSL 21 (1956) | verified | → Quine, W. V. 'Reduction to a dyadic predicate.' 'Universal syntactic translation' (TR-S3-006) is Gemini's phrase. [quine1954reduction] D1 links a ResearchGate page, not the journal. Cite the JSL record. Cobham 1956 is a real follow-up but appears nowhere else in the sources. |

Pairs, Sets and Sequences in First Order Theories (Utrecht) | unverifiable | → Visser, Albert (2008), 'Pairs, sets and sequences in first-order theories', Archive for Mathematical Logic 47(4): 299–326, doi:10.1007/s00153-008-0087-1 (Crossref). Cite the journal version, not the Utrecht dspace preprint. Not read; no card written (outside the PAL debate). Confirm the author and version. | TR-S3-040 | D4 | logical | The only escape from the reduction thesis is an infinite set-theoretic embedding; without it dyads cannot generate triadicity. |  | refuted | → Infinity does no work here. Löwenheim's reduction of ternary and quaternary relatives to binary ones uses the domain of element pairs, n² objects for n individuals, plus binary 'first-member' and 'second-member' relatives V and H. No set theory is involved. On infinite domains no enlargement is needed at all if projection is allowed (Koshkin 2406.14094, Cor. 1). [lowenheim1915moglichkeiten] Probably false as stated. Adding finitely many tuple-objects to a finite domain already lets dyadic relations code a ternary one, so infinity is not what does the work; the extra objects are. Do not use it. |
| TR-S3-041 | D4 | citation | Mentions Quine as a focus of the Triads notebook (no work named). | Quine (no work named) | out_of_scope |  |
| TR-S3-042 | D5 | historical | Quine (1954) argued that polyadic relations can be reduced to a single dyadic predicate J. |  | corrected | → Scope: any interpreted theory written in first-order quantification theory. It translates into a theory Θ′ whose single predicate letter is dyadic. The letter is 'F', never 'J'. F is not set membership. The proof assumes 'a fragment of set theory' and a universe for Θ′ closed under unordered pairing. TR-S3-074 is nearly Quine's wording. TR-S3-015 and TR-S3-049 ('membership as sole primitive') are wrong. The 'three operations' of TR-S3-007 are D1's reconstruction. Quine's own steps are Kuratowski pairs nested to the right, a universe closed under {x, y}, and each atomic sentence coded as nw1;w1;w1;w2;...;w_dn, with F reflexive on that code. [quine1954reduction] Check whether Quine names the predicate J. |
| TR-S3-043 | D5 | citation | Cites Quine 1954 on reduction to a dyadic predicate. | Willard Van Orman Quine (1954) | verified | → Quine, W. V. 'Reduction to a dyadic predicate.' The Journal of Symbolic Logic 19(3): 180-182 (September 1954), doi 10.2307/2268616. Crossref, Cambridge Core and wvquine.org agree, including issue 3. Reprinted in Selected Logic Papers. TR-S3-035's companion, Cobham, 'Reduction to a symmetric predicate', is JSL 21(1): 56-59 (1956), doi 10.2307/2268487. TR-S3-036 is a bare volume link, not a citation. 'Universal syntactic translation' (TR-S3-006) is Gemini's phrase. [quine1954reduction] |
| TR-S3-044 | D5 | logical | Clone theory proves Quine achieved the reduction only by inflating the base ontology to an infinite universe of sets. |  | refuted | → No clone-theory result proves anything about Quine, and the Hereth Correia & Pöschel texts never mention him. Quine's enlargement of the universe is a fact about his construction (p. 180). The published critique of pairing reductions, Koshkin 2022, is an EG/FOL explication argument, not a clone-theory proof, and it grants that Quine is 'correct in a technical algebraic sense' (p. 4). Burch SEP §14 (read directly): 'both Peirce and Quine were correct'. [quine1954reduction] Overclaim. Clone/PAL results prove the reduction thesis within a fixed-universe relational algebra. That Quine's reduction enlarges the universe is a reading of Quine's construction, not a theorem of clone theory. |
| TR-S3-045 | D5 | logical | Quine's reduction smuggles the irreducible ternary branching relation into the pairing operator Pair(x, y, p) and into variable duplication. |  | corrected | → The core charge, that the pairing reduction hides triadic binding, has a published source: Koshkin 2022, p. 2 ('the pairing construction, as formalized in set theory, relies on the very FOL devices that are explicated as using triads') and p. 7 (pairing as a triple junction that 'conceals triadicity'). Corrections: (1) Nothing moves into meta-syntax or 'meta-logic'. Quine defines pairs and identity inside Θ′, and Koshkin puts the hidden triad in object-level FOL variable identification and branch points ('syntactic absorption'). (2) Quine assumes 'a fragment of set theory', not 'the full apparatus of axiomatic set theory' (TR-S3-023). (3) The theorem does not 'fail' (TR-S3-093). Koshkin calls Quine 'correct in a technical algebraic sense' and wrong only 'in a deeper conceptual sense' (p. 4). (4) No Pair(x,y,p) predicate exists in Quine (TR-S3-045). 'Expanding ontology into sets' (TR-S3-024) is right (p. 180). TR-S3-060 keeps its earlier 'corrected' verdict under koshkin2022reduction. Replace 'Burch 2021 SEP, via Koshkin 2024 p. 4' with 'Burch SEP 2021 §14 (read directly); Koshkin 2022 p. 4'. [quine1954reduction] This is Gemini's thesis. 'Irreducible ternary branching relation' needs to be tied to PAL's teridentity with a citation. |
| TR-S3-046 | D6 | rhetorical | The section heading casts Quine's dyadic reduction as a fallacy set against Peirce's Reduction Thesis. |  | out_of_scope | The lab's own text treats Quine's result as valid (peirce/paper.md:39: 'Löwenheim and Quine showed that set-theoretic pairing reduces any relation to dyads'). The 'fallacy' framing is Gemini's, and the talk should not adopt it. |
| TR-S3-047 | D6 | historical | In 1954 Quine proposed an extensional counter-thesis to Peirce's reduction thesis. |  | refuted | → Peirce is not named on p. 180 or in either footnote (pp. 181-182 unread). Quine's n. 1 calls the result 'reminiscent of, but distinct from' Kalmár's and Church-Craig-Quine's, not Peirce's. Koshkin 2022 (n. 1), not 2024, says Quine 'made no mention of Peirce, PRT or what his 1954 reduction to dyads meant for it then, or later', and that 'His silence is usually interpreted as a courteous submission that PRT is false, but he never did say so himself.' The anti-Peirce reading comes from the reception. Koshkin p. 1 counts Löwenheim 1915 and Quine 1954 among the 'counterclaims', and Burch (SEP §14) says the thesis was 'doubted by many, especially after the publication of a proof by Willard Van Orman Quine'. Koshkin's named example of someone who pressed the pairing construction 'as an objection to PRT' is Christopherson & Johnstone, TCSPS 17 (1981): 241-246 (christopherson1981triadicity). Peirce died in 1914, so no 1954 debate took place. Replace every 'Koshkin 2022 (arXiv 2406.14058)' in the locus with 'Koshkin 2022'. [quine1954reduction] See TR-S3-001. |

| TR-S3-060 | G1 t1 | argument_step | Nominalist/descriptive reductions covertly shift irreducible triadic relations into meta-syntax or pairing functions. |  | corrected | → Koshkin (TCSPS 2022) supports this claim. Once branch points and identifications are explicated as pluridentity predicates, the set-theoretic pairing reduction to dyads turns out to use a triple junction, i.e. a teridentity. The mechanism is 'syntactic absorption' of variable identification, not 'meta-syntax', and 'nominalist/descriptive' is Gemini's framing. The register note that Koshkin 'argues the opposite worry' is wrong: he concedes the gerrymandering worry for Herzberger and Burch and then rebuts it. [koshkin2022reduction] A general claim about 'nominalist/descriptive reductions' with no source. Koshkin argues the opposite worry: proofs of the thesis privilege operations. |
| TR-S3-061 | G3 t1 | definition | Characterizes Quine's reduction as an extensional 'Quinean dyadic factorization'. |  | out_of_scope | It attributes to Quine a factorization concept he never proposed. The synergy claim is PID and not computed by the lab. |
| TR-S3-062 | G5 t1 | rhetorical | Quine's dyadic reduction is a 'fallacy' with a pervasive legacy, which the lab confronts. |  | lab_contradicted | The lab does not frame Quine as a fallacy or as a target it confronts. It says Quine 'showed' the reduction and then asks a different question: what a dynamical system's cause-effect structure asserts. |
| TR-S3-063 | G5 t1 | definition | Defines Quine's fallacy as assuming multi-agent dependencies can be modeled by pairwise point-to-point channels. |  | refuted | → Quine 1954 concerns the translatability of interpreted first-order theories. It says nothing about communication channels or multi-agent dependencies, so 'Quine's fallacy' in this sense is invented. [quine1954reduction] Two problems. (1) The attribution to Quine is invented: Quine 1954 is about formal theories, not communication channels (needs_primary if it is kept). (2) The implied claim that pairwise one-input channels cannot model multi-party dependencies runs against the lab: one-input wirings produce adicity-3/4 facts, and pure cycles are Φ = 2.0 wholes. [Refuter overturned 'lab_contradicted': The FINDINGS.md l.20 and ci 'branched wirings=78, min adicity=3' strings are verbatim. However, the lab record is mixed, not contradictory. The same probe finds max cause-side adicity 2 across all 89 one-input wirings: 'dyads compose into chains and cycles and never into joint determination' (paper.md l.277-279). That supports the idea that pairwise channels cannot produce joint multi-party dependency. What the definition attributes to Quine is a textual claim, and it needs Quine 1954.] |
| TR-S3-064 | G5 t1 | citation | Source marker cites the Project doc 'Peirce, Quine, and IIT Synthesis' for the Quine-fallacy framing. | Google Docs: Peirce, Quine, and IIT Synthesis | out_of_scope | This is not a citable source. The framing it supports is the Quine-fallacy one, which the lab does not hold. |

Identity and 'x = {y, z}' are defined within Θ′ from F. The pairing sets are assumed set-theoretically for the interpretation. The 'Quine's Fallacy' heading is Gemini's rhetoric. (The bracketed formulas are inline images in the transcription.) [quine1954reduction] Pair(x1,x2,p) is Gemini's notation. |
