| TR-S3-067 | G5 t1 | rhetorical | Characterizes Quine's reduction as an 'additive' factorization, which post-hoc XAI enforces. |  | out_of_scope | Quine 1954 is not an additive factorization. The label is a misattribution. |
| TR-S3-068 | G6 t1 | historical | Quine (1954) is the canonical text of the dyadic reduction claim, and modern clone theory refutes that claim. | Quine, W. V. O. (1954). Reduction to a Dyadic Predicate. Journal of Symbolic Logic 19(3), 180-182 | refuted | → No clone-theory result proves anything about Quine, and the Hereth Correia & Pöschel texts never mention him. Quine's enlargement of the universe is a fact about his construction (p. 180). The published critique of pairing reductions, Koshkin 2022, is an EG/FOL explication argument, not a clone-theory proof, and it grants that Quine is 'correct in a technical algebraic sense' (p. 4). Burch SEP §14 (read directly): 'both Peirce and Quine were correct'. [quine1954reduction] 'Refutes' is likely wrong. The PAL proofs and Quine's result are compatible because they use different operations and universes (see TR-S3-017 and TR-S3-083). |
| TR-S3-069 | G6 t2 | citation | Quine 1954, 'Reduction to a Dyadic Predicate', The Journal of Symbolic Logic 19(3), 180-182. | Quine, W. V. (1954). Reduction to a Dyadic Predicate. The Journal of Symbolic Logic, 19(3), 180-182 | verified | → Quine, W. V. 'Reduction to a dyadic predicate.' The Journal of Symbolic Logic 19(3): 180-182 (September 1954), doi 10.2307/2268616. Crossref, Cambridge Core and wvquine.org agree, including issue 3. Reprinted in Selected Logic Papers. TR-S3-035's companion, Cobham, 'Reduction to a symmetric predicate', is JSL 21(1): 56-59 (1956), doi 10.2307/2268487. TR-S3-036 is a bare volume link, not a citation. 'Universal syntactic translation' (TR-S3-006) is Gemini's phrase. [quine1954reduction] |
| TR-S3-070 | G6 t3 | historical | Quine's 1954 thesis tries to reduce all polyadic systems to a single dyadic predicate by set-theoretic pairing functions. | Quine (1954) | corrected | → Scope: any interpreted theory written in first-order quantification theory. It translates into a theory Θ′ whose single predicate letter is dyadic. The letter is 'F', never 'J'. F is not set membership. The proof assumes 'a fragment of set theory' and a universe for Θ′ closed under unordered pairing. TR-S3-074 is nearly Quine's wording. TR-S3-015 and TR-S3-049 ('membership as sole primitive') are wrong. The 'three operations' of TR-S3-007 are D1's reconstruction. Quine's own steps are Kuratowski pairs nested to the right, a universe closed under {x, y}, and each atomic sentence coded as nw1;w1;w1;w2;...;w_dn, with F reflexive on that code. [quine1954reduction] 'Tries to' and 'polyadic systems' are Gemini's wording. |
| TR-S3-071 | G6 t3 | argument_step | Quine's reduction relies on an infinite set-theoretic universe (ontological inflation). | Quine (1954) | corrected | → Quine does enlarge the universe. Θ′ ranges over Θ's objects plus {x, y} for every x and y in Θ′, so the universe is closed under unordered pairing. He assumes only a set-theory fragment that guarantees {x, y} and that x differs from {x, y} and {{x}}. The paper mentions no ∅, no 'inductive set universe', and never uses the word 'infinite'. On a standard well-founded reading the closure is infinite, but Quine does not say so. He adds that Θ's universe may already contain the pairs, in which case nothing is added. 'Solely' and 'only' overstate. TR-S3-029's 'meta-theoretic ontology' is wrong: the pairs belong to Θ′'s object-level universe. TR-S3-018 and TR-S3-102 are correct: the translation does not stay within Θ's universe. For TR-S3-017, attribute the reconciliation to Burch (2021, SEP, quoted in Koshkin 2024 p. 4): 'both Peirce and Quine were correct: the issue entirely depends on exactly what constructive resources are to be allowed'. [quine1954reduction] 'Infinite' is unverified; see TR-S3-040. |
| TR-S3-072 | G6 t3 | argument_step | Quine's reduction smuggles the triadic teridentity relation into the meta-syntax of coordinate binding. | Quine (1954) | corrected | → A published source makes the substance of this claim: Koshkin 2022, not Quine's text and not Burch. Koshkin p. 2: 'the pairing construction, as formalized in set theory, relies on the very FOL devices that are explicated as using triads.' On p. 7 he writes the pairing step as a triple junction ∃t[R(1)(t,x1) ∧ R(2)(t,x2) ∧ R̂(t,x3)] that 'conceals triadicity'. PAL simulates a triple junction with the teridentity I3 (p. 5, Fig. 2a), so in Koshkin's explication the pairing reduction uses a teridentity. Four corrections. (a) Quine has no triadic Pair(x,y,p) primitive, and Koshkin's device is an existential pair-element plus three dyads. (b) The mechanism is 'syntactic absorption' of object-level variable identification and branch points, not 'meta-syntax' or 'meta-linguistic coordinate binding'. (c) The argument is an EG/FOL explication, not a 'universal-algebraic' or clone-theoretic proof, and 'join-irreducible' is not Koshkin's term (TR-S3-081, -104). (d) Koshkin speaks of 'the set-theoretic pairing construction' and 'pairing reductions a la Löwenheim and Quine' (p. 4), not of Quine's formula (1). Christopherson & Johnstone 1981 argued on the other side: per Koshkin they pressed pairing against PRT. They do not support this claim. [koshkin2022reduction] This is Gemini's thesis with no source. |
| TR-S3-073 | G6 t3 | argument_step | The PAL/IIT/ΦID formalisms mathematically refute Quinean reductionism. |  | refuted | → Neither IIT nor ΦID addresses Quine's theorem. PAL results are compatible with it (Burch 2021). No formalism 'refutes' a valid translatability theorem. Overclaim. The lab makes no such claim: peirce/paper.md:39–49 treats the logic debate as settled in its own terms and says the lab asks a different question. IIT and ΦID do not address Quine's theorem. |

It translates into a theory Θ′ whose single predicate letter is dyadic. The letter is 'F', never 'J'. F is not set membership. The proof assumes 'a fragment of set theory' and a universe for Θ′ closed under unordered pairing. TR-S3-074 is nearly Quine's wording. TR-S3-015 and TR-S3-049 ('membership as sole primitive') are wrong. The 'three operations' of TR-S3-007 are D1's reconstruction. Quine's own steps are Kuratowski pairs nested to the right, a universe closed under {x, y}, and each atomic sentence coded as nw1;w1;w1;w2;...;w_dn, with F reflexive on that code. [quine1954reduction] 'Interpreted theory in quantification theory' may be close to Quine's wording. Quote it directly if it is. |
| TR-S3-075 | G6 t4 | argument_step | Quine's reduction assumes set theory can construct ordered pairs and sets. | Quine (1954) | verified | → Correct, with a qualification. Quine assumes a fragment of set theory, not full set theory, and states the assumption openly. [quine1954reduction] |
| TR-S3-076 | G6 t4 | argument_step | Quine's reduction assumes ordered tuples can structurally encode multi-place predicates. | Quine (1954) | verified | → Right-nested, as D1 says: x;(y;z). The atomic code also carries the predicate's index: x = nw1;w1;w1;w2;w3;...;w_dn, where nw is Quine's name for w;...;w with n terms. TR-S3-077 is G6's recap and should not be quoted as Quine's wording. [quine1954reduction] |

The atomic code also carries the predicate's index: x = nw1;w1;w1;w2;w3;...;w_dn, where nw is Quine's name for w;...;w with n terms. TR-S3-077 is G6's recap and should not be quoted as Quine's wording. [quine1954reduction] This is G6's argument recap, not Quine's wording. |
| TR-S3-078 | G6 t4 | argument_step | Premise 2: one dyadic predicate, read as set membership/pairing, captures these tuple structures. | Quine (1954) | corrected | → F is not set membership. Only its first disjunct is membership-like: (∃z)(x = {y, z}), so y belongs to the pair-set x. The second disjunct makes F reflexive on codes of true atomic sentences. There is no 'J'. [quine1954reduction] 'Set membership/pairing' hedges between two different readings. |

Quine's own steps are Kuratowski pairs nested to the right, a universe closed under {x, y}, and each atomic sentence coded as nw1;w1;w1;w2;...;w_dn, with F reflexive on that code. On p. 7 he writes the pairing step as a triple junction ∃t[R(1)(t,x1) ∧ R(2)(t,x2) ∧ R̂(t,x3)] that 'conceals triadicity'. PAL simulates a triple junction with the teridentity I3 (p. 5, Fig. 2a), so in Koshkin's explication the pairing reduction uses a teridentity. Four corrections. (a) Quine has no triadic Pair(x,y,p) primitive, and Koshkin's device is an existential pair-element plus three dyads. (b) The mechanism is 'syntactic absorption' of object-level variable identification and branch points, not 'meta-syntax' or 'meta-linguistic coordinate binding'. (c) The argument is an EG/FOL explication, not a 'universal-algebraic' or clone-theoretic proof, and 'join-irreducible' is not Koshkin's term (TR-S3-081, -104). (d) Koshkin speaks of 'the set-theoretic pairing construction' and 'pairing reductions a la Löwenheim and Quine' (p. 4), not of Quine's formula (1). | TR-S3-082 | G6 t6 | argument_step | Hereth Correia & Pöschel's result refutes Quinean reduction within closed relational algebra. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2006) | refuted | → No clone-theory result proves anything about Quine, and the Hereth Correia & Pöschel texts never mention him. It still needs a logician's own statement. |

It translates into a theory Θ′ whose single predicate letter is dyadic. The letter is 'F', never 'J'. F is not set membership. The proof assumes 'a fragment of set theory' and a universe for Θ′ closed under unordered pairing. TR-S3-074 is nearly Quine's wording. | TR-S3-085 | G2 t2 | historical | Peirce's trichotomy and Quine's 'program of dyadic reduction' stand in historical conflict, and that conflict turns on whether a branching node is necessary. |  | corrected | → Quine 1954 says nothing of branching. The framing is not only Gemini's: Koshkin 2022 sets Peirce against Quine ('It cannot simply be that Peirce and Quine are both right', p. 4) and ties the question to the triple junction that bonding disallows (p. 4) and that pairing conceals (p. 7, eq. 5). 'Historical conflict' still overstates, since Quine never engaged the thesis (n. 1). Call it a conflict in the later literature. [quine1954reduction] The branching point is well grounded in the PAL literature (and in the lab's summary of it, peirce/paper.md:41–43, which cannot serve as evidence for the texts). Casting it as a Peirce–Quine 'historical conflict' is Gemini's framing. |
| TR-S3-086 | G2 t2 | logical | Quine's reduction covertly smuggles in triadic branching through meta-linguistic coordinate binding (Gemini marker [1]). |  | corrected | → The core charge, that the pairing reduction hides triadic binding, has a published source: Koshkin 2022, p. 2 ('the pairing construction, as formalized in set theory, relies on the very FOL devices that are explicated as using triads') and p. 7 (pairing as a triple junction that 'conceals triadicity'). Corrections: (1) Nothing moves into meta-syntax or 'meta-logic'. Quine defines pairs and identity inside Θ′, and Koshkin puts the hidden triad in object-level FOL variable identification and branch points ('syntactic absorption'). (2) Quine assumes 'a fragment of set theory', not 'the full apparatus of axiomatic set theory' (TR-S3-023). (3) The theorem does not 'fail' (TR-S3-093). Koshkin calls Quine 'correct in a technical algebraic sense' and wrong only 'in a deeper conceptual sense' (p. 4). (4) No Pair(x,y,p) predicate exists in Quine (TR-S3-045). 'Expanding ontology into sets' (TR-S3-024) is right (p. 180). TR-S3-060 keeps its earlier 'corrected' verdict under koshkin2022reduction. Replace 'Burch 2021 SEP, via Koshkin 2024 p. 4' with 'Burch SEP 2021 §14 (read directly); Koshkin 2022 p. 4'. [quine1954reduction] This is Gemini's thesis with no source. |

| TR-S3-091 | P1 | historical | The thesis passed through a 1954 dyadic reduction debate with W.V.O. Quine. |  | refuted | → Peirce is not named on p. 180 or in either footnote (pp. 181-182 unread). Quine's n. 1 calls the result 'reminiscent of, but distinct from' Kalmár's and Church-Craig-Quine's, not Peirce's. Koshkin 2022 (n. 1), not 2024, says Quine 'made no mention of Peirce, PRT or what his 1954 reduction to dyads meant for it then, or later', and that 'His silence is usually interpreted as a courteous submission that PRT is false, but he never did say so himself.' The anti-Peirce reading comes from the reception. Koshkin p. 1 counts Löwenheim 1915 and Quine 1954 among the 'counterclaims', and Burch (SEP §14) says the thesis was 'doubted by many, especially after the publication of a proof by Willard Van Orman Quine'. Koshkin's named example of someone who pressed the pairing construction 'as an objection to PRT' is Christopherson & Johnstone, TCSPS 17 (1981): 241-246 (christopherson1981triadicity). Peirce died in 1914, so no 1954 debate took place. Replace every 'Koshkin 2022 (arXiv 2406.14058)' in the locus with 'Koshkin 2022'. [quine1954reduction] There was no 1954 debate with Quine (Peirce died in 1914). See TR-S3-054. |

| TR-S3-098 | P1 | argument_step | Quine did not refute Peirce. |  | corrected | → The claim is defensible on two grounds. (1) Quine never claimed a refutation. [quine1954reduction] Defensible only as 'the two results concern different operations and universes'. P1 instead rests it on a 'Quinean Fallacy', which the talk should not use. |
| TR-S3-099 | P1 | logical | Quine's syntactic proof relies on Wiener-Kuratowski ordered pairs. |  | corrected | → Quine names Kuratowski's pair {{x}, {x, y}}. 'Wiener-Kuratowski' (TR-S3-031, TR-S3-099, TR-S3-100) conflates two definitions. Quine does not use Wiener's. The text read contains no claim 'to avoid ternary primitives' (TR-S3-022). TR-S3-051 is right in substance: Θ′'s universe is closed under unordered pairs {x, y}, and so under Kuratowski pairs. [quine1954reduction] 'Wiener-Kuratowski' conflates two pair definitions. |
| TR-S3-100 | P1 | definition | The ordered pair used is <x1,x2> = {{x1},{x1,x2}}. |  | corrected | → Quine names Kuratowski's pair {{x}, {x, y}}. 'Wiener-Kuratowski' (TR-S3-031, TR-S3-099, TR-S3-100) conflates two definitions. Quine does not use Wiener's. The text read contains no claim 'to avoid ternary primitives' (TR-S3-022). TR-S3-051 is right in substance: Θ′'s universe is closed under unordered pairs {x, y}, and so under Kuratowski pairs. [quine1954reduction] {{x1},{x1,x2}} is Kuratowski's pair, not Wiener's ({{{a},∅},{{b}}}). P1 mislabels it 'Wiener-Kuratowski'. |
| TR-S3-101 | P1 | logical | Quine's proof relies on inflating the domain to an infinite set-theoretic universe X*. |  | corrected | → Quine does enlarge the universe. Θ′ ranges over Θ's objects plus {x, y} for every x and y in Θ′, so the universe is closed under unordered pairing. He assumes only a set-theory fragment that guarantees {x, y} and that x differs from {x, y} and {{x}}. The paper mentions no ∅, no 'inductive set universe', and never uses the word 'infinite'. On a standard well-founded reading the closure is infinite, but Quine does not say so. He adds that Θ's universe may already contain the pairs, in which case nothing is added. 'Solely' and 'only' overstate. TR-S3-029's 'meta-theoretic ontology' is wrong: the pairs belong to Θ′'s object-level universe. TR-S3-018 and TR-S3-102 are correct: the translation does not stay within Θ's universe. For TR-S3-017, attribute the reconciliation to Burch (2021, SEP, quoted in Koshkin 2024 p. 4): 'both Peirce and Quine were correct: the issue entirely depends on exactly what constructive resources are to be allowed'. [quine1954reduction] 'Infinite' is unverified; see TR-S3-040. |
| TR-S3-102 | P1 | argument_step | Quine does not eliminate triadic relations within the domain X. |  | corrected | → Quine does enlarge the universe. Θ′ ranges over Θ's objects plus {x, y} for every x and y in Θ′, so the universe is closed under unordered pairing. He assumes only a set-theory fragment that guarantees {x, y} and that x differs from {x, y} and {{x}}. The paper mentions no ∅, no 'inductive set universe', and never uses the word 'infinite'. On a standard well-founded reading the closure is infinite, but Quine does not say so. He adds that Θ's universe may already contain the pairs, in which case nothing is added. 'Solely' and 'only' overstate. TR-S3-029's 'meta-theoretic ontology' is wrong: the pairs belong to Θ′'s object-level universe. TR-S3-018 and TR-S3-102 are correct: the translation does not stay within Θ's universe. For TR-S3-017, attribute the reconciliation to Burch (2021, SEP, quoted in Koshkin 2024 p. 4): 'both Peirce and Quine were correct: the issue entirely depends on exactly what constructive resources are to be allowed'. [quine1954reduction] |
| TR-S3-103 | P1 | argument_step | Quine externalizes coordinate binding into meta-syntax. |  | corrected | → The core charge, that the pairing reduction hides triadic binding, has a published source: Koshkin 2022, p. 2 ('the pairing construction, as formalized in set theory, relies on the very FOL devices that are explicated as using triads') and p. 7 (pairing as a triple junction that 'conceals triadicity'). Corrections: (1) Nothing moves into meta-syntax or 'meta-logic'. Quine defines pairs and identity inside Θ′, and Koshkin puts the hidden triad in object-level FOL variable identification and branch points ('syntactic absorption'). (2) Quine assumes 'a fragment of set theory', not 'the full apparatus of axiomatic set theory' (TR-S3-023). (3) The theorem does not 'fail' (TR-S3-093). Koshkin calls Quine 'correct in a technical algebraic sense' and wrong only 'in a deeper conceptual sense' (p. 4). (4) No Pair(x,y,p) predicate exists in Quine (TR-S3-045). 'Expanding ontology into sets' (TR-S3-024) is right (p. 180). TR-S3-060 keeps its earlier 'corrected' verdict under koshkin2022reduction. Replace 'Burch 2021 SEP, via Koshkin 2024 p. 4' with 'Burch SEP 2021 §14 (read directly); Koshkin 2022 p. 4'. [quine1954reduction] This is Gemini's thesis. The new objects live in the object-level domain, not the meta-syntax. |
| TR-S3-104 | P1 | argument_step | Quine's set-theoretic reduction covertly smuggles in the join-irreducible teridentity relation =_3. |  | corrected | → A published source makes the substance of this claim: Koshkin 2022, not Quine's text and not Burch. Koshkin p. 2: 'the pairing construction, as formalized in set theory, relies on the very FOL devices that are explicated as using triads.' On p. 7 he writes the pairing step as a triple junction ∃t[R(1)(t,x1) ∧ R(2)(t,x2) ∧ R̂(t,x3)] that 'conceals triadicity'. PAL simulates a triple junction with the teridentity I3 (p. 5, Fig. 2a), so in Koshkin's explication the pairing reduction uses a teridentity. Four corrections. (a) Quine has no triadic Pair(x,y,p) primitive, and Koshkin's device is an existential pair-element plus three dyads. (b) The mechanism is 'syntactic absorption' of object-level variable identification and branch points, not 'meta-syntax' or 'meta-linguistic coordinate binding'. (c) The argument is an EG/FOL explication, not a 'universal-algebraic' or clone-theoretic proof, and 'join-irreducible' is not Koshkin's term (TR-S3-081, -104). (d) Koshkin speaks of 'the set-theoretic pairing construction' and 'pairing reductions a la Löwenheim and Quine' (p. 4), not of Quine's formula (1). Christopherson & Johnstone 1981 argued on the other side: per Koshkin they pressed pairing against PRT. They do not support this claim. [koshkin2022reduction] This is Gemini's thesis with no source. |
| TR-S3-105 | P1 | argument_step | The smuggling occurs through variable replication across conjuncts and a triadic pairing relation Pair(x1,x2,p). |  | corrected | → A published source makes the substance of this claim: Koshkin 2022, not Quine's text and not Burch. Koshkin p. 2: 'the pairing construction, as formalized in set theory, relies on the very FOL devices that are explicated as using triads.' On p. 7 he writes the pairing step as a triple junction ∃t[R(1)(t,x1) ∧ R(2)(t,x2) ∧ R̂(t,x3)] that 'conceals triadicity'. PAL simulates a triple junction with the teridentity I3 (p. 5, Fig. 2a), so in Koshkin's explication the pairing reduction uses a teridentity. Four corrections. (a) Quine has no triadic Pair(x,y,p) primitive, and Koshkin's device is an existential pair-element plus three dyads. (b) The mechanism is 'syntactic absorption' of object-level variable identification and branch points, not 'meta-syntax' or 'meta-linguistic coordinate binding'. (c) The argument is an EG/FOL explication, not a 'universal-algebraic' or clone-theoretic proof, and 'join-irreducible' is not Koshkin's term (TR-S3-081, -104). (d) Koshkin speaks of 'the set-theoretic pairing construction' and 'pairing reductions a la Löwenheim and Quine' (p. 4), not of Quine's formula (1). Christopherson & Johnstone 1981 argued on the other side: per Koshkin they pressed pairing against PRT. They do not support this claim. [koshkin2022reduction] |
| TR-S3-106 | P1 | argument_step | Quine's reduction commits a syntactic use/mention fallacy. |  | corrected | → The earlier note, that no source charges a use/mention error, is wrong. Koshkin 2022, p. 2: 'proposed reductions to dyads, when taken in anything other than a narrow technical sense, emerge as instances of the use/mention error pointed out by Peirce in connection with dyadic algebra'. The reductions meant are the pairing reductions 'a la Löwenheim and Quine' (p. 4). Correct 'fallacy' to 'error', add Koshkin's limit (only beyond the narrow technical sense), and attribute the charge to Koshkin, not Quine. Quine's construction itself is stated in the object language of Θ′. TR-S5-114's clause 'that destroys intrinsic cause-effect power' has no source and stays lab-contradicted (dyadic-decomposition-phi-zero). Koshkin's own quotation of CP 8.331 ('it itself employs') differs from Peirce's wording ('it does itself employ'; see quote-cp8331 group). [koshkin2022reduction] This appears to be Gemini's invention. No known PAL paper charges Quine with a use/mention error. Drop it. |
| TR-S3-107 | P1 | logical | Quine maps a polyadic relation R(x1,x2,y) to a dyadic statement J(<x1,x2>,y). |  | refuted | → Neither J(<x1..xn>, c_R) nor J(<x1,x2>, y) is Quine's. F has two disjuncts. Either x is a pair-set containing y, or x = y and x codes a true atomic sentence F_n w1...w_dn. So F_n w1...w_dn holds exactly when F relates the code nw1;w1;w1;w2;...;w_dn to itself. The first disjunct cannot fire when x = y, because Quine assumes x ≠ {x, z}. His explicit definitions inside Θ′ are on pp. 181-182, which remain unread. [quine1954reduction] This conflicts with D1's J(<x1..xn>, c_R) (TR-S3-013). |
| TR-S3-108 | P1 | definition | Quine's transformation uses Kuratowski pairing <x1,x2> = {{x1},{x1,x2}}. |  | corrected | → Quine names Kuratowski's pair {{x}, {x, y}}. 'Wiener-Kuratowski' (TR-S3-031, TR-S3-099, TR-S3-100) conflates two definitions. Quine does not use Wiener's. The text read contains no claim 'to avoid ternary primitives' (TR-S3-022). TR-S3-051 is right in substance: Θ′'s universe is closed under unordered pairs {x, y}, and so under Kuratowski pairs. [quine1954reduction] |

## S4 — The modern logic and philosophy debate (213)

| id | source | kind | claim | cited work | status | note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TR-S4-001 | D1 | historical | Universal algebra, PAL and relational clone theory were developed by Burch (1991), Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004, 2006) and Koshkin (2024). | Burch 1991; Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2004, 2006; Koshkin 2024 | corrected | → Koshkin did not develop universal algebra, PAL or relational clone theory. He credits co-clones to Post and the Pol-Inv tradition, and PAL to Burch. In the 2023/24 paper he unifies database joins, the co-clone (projoin) algebra and Peirce's bond algebra, and says he avoids the Pol-Inv Galois connection. Only the 2025 paper works through functional completeness and co-clone bases. Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2004 connected PAL to clones. 'Dau (2006)' is Hereth Correia & Dau 2006, LNCS 3874. The Hereth Correia, Pöschel and Dau rows belong to other clusters. [koshkin2024reduction] Overclaims: universal algebra and relational clone theory predate all of these authors (Post; Geiger 1968; Bodnarčuk et al. 1969; Pöschel & Kalužnin 1979). Only PAL is Burch's. The lab's own paper cites Hereth & Pöschel 2011 (Semiotica 186) as the strongest proof, which D1 never names. |
| TR-S4-002 | D1 | logical | These authors have proven that Quine's reduction fails within any closed relational universe. |  | refuted | → Keep the existing correction. The closest published position to a 'Quinean Fallacy' is Koshkin 2022: pairing reductions taken 'in anything other than a narrow technical sense' are 'instances of the use/mention error' (p. 2), while Quine is 'correct in a technical algebraic sense' (p. 4). No one proves the reduction 'fails' or is 'invalid'. [quine1954reduction] No cited author 'proves Quine's reduction fails'. Quine's theorem holds; the PAL results concern a different setting (fixed domain, no branching without teridentity). Rephrase as 'the two results answer different questions.' |
| TR-S4-003 | D1 | logical | Under primitive positive logic over a fixed domain, teridentity is join-irreducible. |  | refuted | → 'Join-irreducible' has 0 hits in HCP 2004, HCP 2006, Dau & HC 2006, HP 2011 and the three Koshkin papers. In the lattice of relational clones, ⟨{=3}⟩_RA = ⟨∅⟩_RA = D_A (HCP 2004 §3) is the bottom element, so the lattice term does not apply. The confusion is probably with PAL's operation named 'join' (δ^{i,j}). The theorem is that id3 cannot be built by PAL operations, joins included, from unary and binary relations without id3 (HCP 2006 Thm 2). [herethcorreia2006teridentity] 'Join-irreducible' looks like Gemini's term. In lattice theory it means an element that is not a join of two smaller ones. In the lattice of relational clones, the clone generated by =3 is the trivial clone that equality already generates, so the term is misapplied. The literature proves that teridentity cannot be constructed in PAL without branching. |
| TR-S4-004 | D1 | argument_step | The Peirce-Quine debate turns on set-theoretic ontological reduction vs algebraic closure in a fixed domain. |  | stipulation | A defensible framing, but keep it as the talk's claim and do not attribute it to the logicians. |
| TR-S4-005 | D1 | citation | Burch (1991) formalized PAL and proved Peirce's Reduction Thesis. | Burch 1991, A Peircean Reduction Thesis | corrected | → As reported by HCP 2006 and Dau & HC 2006 (Burch 1991 not read), Burch proved the thesis in PAL under a juxtaposition restriction, which is not a 'clone restriction'. Burch's own SEP summary says the proof was 'accomplished in 1988 (Burch 1991)' and omits the restriction. 'First algebraic proof' (132, 199) is wrong. Herzberger 1981 proves T7 (lower polyads absolutely irreducible) and T8 (the reduction thesis holds within any sufficiently large domain) in his bonding algebra; both are matched in two scans. Conarroe reports that Burch 1997 p. 251 claims to drop Herzberger's domain-size stipulation (unread). [burch1991reduction] The lab's references.bib gives Texas Tech University Press, Lubbock, 1991. Herzberger 1981 proved an earlier version (lab paper.md), so 'first' claims need care. |
| TR-S4-006 | D1 | citation | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004, 2006) refined PAL. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2004, 2006 | verified | → Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2004 (LNAI 2961) and 2006 (LNAI 4068) modified PAL by allowing juxtaposition anywhere and using one join operator. Dau & Hereth Correia 2006 (LNAI 3874) is the third paper in the group. [herethcorreia2004power] |
| TR-S4-007 | D1 | citation | Dau (2006) and Koshkin (2024), with Hereth Correia & Pöschel, translated PAL into universal algebra and clone theory. | Dau 2006; Koshkin 2024 | corrected | → Koshkin did not develop universal algebra, PAL or relational clone theory. He credits co-clones to Post and the Pol-Inv tradition, and PAL to Burch. In the 2023/24 paper he unifies database joins, the co-clone (projoin) algebra and Peirce's bond algebra, and says he avoids the Pol-Inv Galois connection. Only the 2025 paper works through functional completeness and co-clone bases. Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2004 connected PAL to clones. 'Dau (2006)' is Hereth Correia & Dau 2006, LNCS 3874. The Hereth Correia, Pöschel and Dau rows belong to other clusters. [koshkin2024reduction] The 'Dau (2006)' here is actually Dau & Hereth Correia (a co-authored paper). Koshkin's framework is database joins and bond algebra, not clone theory as such. |
| TR-S4-008 | D1 | definition | Rel(A), the union of P(A^m), is the set of all finitary relations on A. |  | stipulation |  |
| TR-S4-009 | D1 | definition | A relational clone (co-clone) is a set of relations containing the binary identity Δ_A. |  | corrected | → HCP 2004 Def. 2 defines a relational algebra (called a relational clone for finite A) by closure under (R1) the diagonal Δ_A, (R2a) cyclic shift, (R2b) transposition, (R3) identification of the first two coordinates, (R4) relational product and (R5) adding a fictitious coordinate. Theorem 6 makes this equivalent to closure under ∃,∧,= formulas (pp). 009 is incomplete. 010 is right in substance. 011 wrongly lists intersection as basic: it is derived (R12), and identification is basic. [herethcorreia2004power] Incomplete definition. A relational clone must also be closed under pp-definitions, and it then contains every diagonal relation, teridentity included. |
| TR-S4-010 | D1 | definition | A relational clone is closed under pp logic: product, permutation, identification of variables, projection. |  | corrected | → HCP 2004 Def. 2 defines a relational algebra (called a relational clone for finite A) by closure under (R1) the diagonal Δ_A, (R2a) cyclic shift, (R2b) transposition, (R3) identification of the first two coordinates, (R4) relational product and (R5) adding a fictitious coordinate. Theorem 6 makes this equivalent to closure under ∃,∧,= formulas (pp). 009 is incomplete. 010 is right in substance. 011 wrongly lists intersection as basic: it is derived (R12), and identification is basic. [herethcorreia2004power] Correct in substance. Closure under identification of variables is exactly what makes =3 pp-definable (x=y ∧ y=z), and that undercuts the D1 rows claiming that =3 lies outside the dyadic pp-clone. |
| TR-S4-011 | D1 | definition | Listed clone operations R1-R4 are permutation, projection, intersection, product (intersection listed in place of identification). |  | corrected | → HCP 2004 Def. 2 defines a relational algebra (called a relational clone for finite A) by closure under (R1) the diagonal Δ_A, (R2a) cyclic shift, (R2b) transposition, (R3) identification of the first two coordinates, (R4) relational product and (R5) adding a fictitious coordinate. Theorem 6 makes this equivalent to closure under ∃,∧,= formulas (pp). 009 is incomplete. 010 is right in substance. 011 wrongly lists intersection as basic: it is derived (R12), and identification is basic. [herethcorreia2004power] The list of operations is garbled: it gives intersection where identification of variables belongs. Rebuild it from the primary definition. |
| TR-S4-012 | D1 | definition | Teridentity =3 = {(x,x,x) : x in A} is the central operator of the algebraic framework. |  | stipulation | =3 is a relation, not an 'operator'. |
| TR-S4-013 | D1 | historical | Burch, Hereth Correia and Pöschel proved two invariant results. |  | corrected | → The two results come under different operation sets and conditions. Irreducibility holds in PAL without teridentity for any A with \|A\|≥2 (HCP 2006 Thm 2). Constructibility holds in PAL with teridentity and complement on finite A, plus a pairing bijection on infinite A (HP 2011 §3). Burch proved both under his juxtaposition restriction. Do not package them as 'invariant' results. [hereth2011logic] 'Two invariant results' is Gemini's packaging. Check that each clause matches a stated theorem. |
| TR-S4-014 | D1 | logical | Dyadic incompleteness: for \|A\| >= 2, the pp-clone generated by all monadic and dyadic relations is strictly incomplete; =3 is not in it. |  | refuted | → =3 ∈ ⟨Rel^(≤2)(A)⟩_pp for every A. Every relational algebra contains all diagonal relations (⟨∅⟩_RA = D_A, HCP 2004 §3), and =3 = ∆(ζ(= × =)) (Dau & HC p.108). The true theorem is id3 ∉ ⟨Rel^(≤2)(A)⟩_PAL∖{id3}, PAL without teridentity, which allows products, two-place joins, complement and permutation but not coordinate identification (HCP 2006 Thm 2, \|A\|≥2; HCP 2004 Thm 13 for finite A). 'Restricted pp logic' (109) should be stated as PAL without teridentity. [dau2006instances] Likely false as stated in standard co-clone semantics. Every relational clone contains all diagonal relations; =3 is pp-definable as x=y ∧ y=z; and for \|A\| >= 3 the binary relations (all unary relations plus ≠) already pp-generate Rel(A). The true theorem concerns PAL operations, which join lines two places at a time with no branching. State it in PAL terms. |
| TR-S4-015 | D1 | logical | Ternary completeness: the clone generated by relations of arity <= 3 is all of Rel(A). |  | corrected | → In the co-clone (primitive positive) sense, relations of arity ≤ 3 generate all relations on every domain (Koshkin 2025, Thm 2). On a finite domain one ternary Sheffer relation suffices, and for \|D\| ≥ 3 binaries suffice. In the bond (Peirce/PAL) sense, 'everything is built from teridentities and dyads' holds on infinite domains (Koshkin 2024 Thm 14) and on finite domains with \|D\| ≥ 3. It fails on 2-element domains, where ¬I3 is not a bond of binaries and teridentities, although arities ≥ 4 still reduce to ternaries there. 'Subcubic hypergraph networks' should read subcubic graphs. The register's note that 'Koshkin reports reduction to triads fails on finite domains' is refuted. [koshkin2025completeness] The outcome depends on the setting. In the co-clone sense on a finite A it is plausible: on {0,1}, a single ternary relation such as 1-in-3 generates everything. In PAL/bond settings, Koshkin reports that reduction to triads fails on finite domains (see rows 125/126). |
| TR-S4-016 | D1 | logical | Adjoining teridentity alone to all dyadic relations generates all finitary relations. |  | corrected | → Theorem 13(a) holds for finite A with teridentity and negation. Theorem 13(b)'s positive claim ⟨R2⟩_pPAL = ⟨Rel^(≤2)(A)⟩_RA = Rel(A) conflicts with koshkin2025completeness Theorem 4 for \|A\| = 2. On {0,1} the pp closure of the binary relations is the bijunctive co-clone, and ¬I3 is 'non-bijunctive, and hence irreducible to binary relations on Boolean domains'. The equality holds for finite \|A\| ≥ 3. Koshkin does not flag the conflict. Do not state the positive (pp) thesis for \|A\| = 2 without this caveat. The strict inclusions, which HP 2006 and HP 2011 prove, are unaffected. [herethcorreia2004power] Doubtful as stated. On {0,1}, all binary relations plus =3 pp-generate only the bijunctive (2-CNF) co-clone, which excludes x⊕y⊕z=0. Peirce's constructive clause uses triadic relations in general, not teridentity alone. |
| TR-S4-017 | D1 | logical | In cylindric/polyadic algebras variable duplication is handled by the diagonal element d_123 = {(x,x,x)}, i.e. teridentity. |  | unverifiable | → No cylindric- or polyadic-algebra source (Henkin–Monk–Tarski 1971; Halmos 1962) was read. By the standard definitions, cylindric algebras have binary diagonal elements d_ij and the ternary diagonal is the meet d_12·d_23, not a primitive d_123. Check against Henkin–Monk–Tarski before use, or drop. Cylindric algebras have binary diagonals d_ij and no primitive 'd_123'. The ternary diagonal is the meet d_12·d_23, which is the opposite of what the row suggests. |

| TR-S4-031 | D1 | citation | Abramsky & Brandenburger 2011 and Abramsky, Barbosa, Searle 2024 formalize contextuality as obstruction to global sections. | Abramsky and Brandenburger 2011; Abramsky, Barbosa, and Searle 2024 | corrected | → Verified in substance: contextuality and non-locality 'correspond exactly to obstructions to the existence of global sections' (A&B 2011 abstract; Theorem 8.1). ABS 2024 carries the same criterion over to a presheaf of strategies (p. 8). 'Topological obstruction' is not A&B's phrase. The cohomological version is Abramsky, Mansfield & Barbosa 2012, which A&B report on p. 29. TR-S4-183's 'in quantum systems' overstates the result: specific models (Bell, Hardy, GHZ) lack global sections, not every quantum model. [abramsky2011structure] Peripheral to the talk. |
| TR-S4-032 | D1 | historical | The sheaf-theoretic work explicitly analyzes XOR and Bell-type games. |  | corrected | → A&B 2011 analyses Bell, Hardy and GHZ models, PR boxes and Kochen–Specker configurations, but the strings 'XOR' and 'game' do not occur in it. ABS 2024 frames contextuality as an Experimenter–Nature game and mentions non-local games (p. 1). Its only XOR is classical processing in a GHZ/MBQC example (p. 4), not an XOR game. -185's 'almost exclusively physical' is half right: the setting is 'completely independent of Hilbert space' (p. 2), and maximal contextuality equals CSP unsolvability (Prop. 6.4). [abramsky2011structure] Peripheral. |
| TR-S4-033 | D1 | historical | Category-theoretic logic has shown lines of identity and relational duplicators form hypergraph categories and Frobenius algebras. |  | corrected | → The Frobenius half is right. Haydon & Sobociński (2020) draw Peirce's Beta existential graphs as string diagrams. In them, 'lines of identity obey the laws of special Frobenius algebras' (p. 1), and the negation-free rules are the axioms of cartesian bicategories of relations. The three-way copy/merge node is Peirce's teridentity, and the paper says so (p. 8). Three corrections. (1) 'Hypergraph categories' comes from Fong & Spivak's 'Graphical Regular Logic', not from Haydon & Sobociński. In Prop. 3.15, Rel of a regular category is a hypergraph category whose Frobenius maps are the diagonal x ⊆ x × x × x. (2) The Frobenius laws are axioms imposed on the generators that draw lines of identity. The papers show that Peirce's rules match those axioms and that Rel_X satisfies them. (3) None of the three papers engages Peirce's reduction thesis. Each takes the three-way node as a primitive generator (Bonchi et al. call the copier a 'fundamental constant', p. 4). None asks whether it could be derived from dyads. [haydon2020compositional] Unattributed. Probably D1's works-cited item 38 (row 061). [metadata only: the work exists as cited; its text was not read] |
| TR-S4-034 | D1 | argument_step | The sheaf/category literature is quantum-focused and not connected to relational clones, PAL, or IIT's MIP. |  | corrected | → Neither A&B 2011 nor ABS 2024 mentions clones, PAL, teridentity, PID/ΦID or IIT (grep of both: 0 hits). Both do connect contextuality to relational structure: A&B equate maximal contextuality with CSP unsolvability (Prop. 6.4), and ABS list 'database theory, constraint satisfaction' among the approach's connections (p. 2). So 'not connected' needs the narrower wording. The wider literature was not searched. [abramsky2011structure] A negative literature claim, and weakened by the fact that the same framework treats relational models (possibilistic contextuality). Avoid it. |
| TR-S4-035 | D1 | logical | PAL proves there is no primitive fourthness; every n-ary relation is built from teridentities and dyads. |  | corrected | → In the co-clone (primitive positive) sense, relations of arity ≤ 3 generate all relations on every domain (Koshkin 2025, Thm 2). On a finite domain one ternary Sheffer relation suffices, and for \|D\| ≥ 3 binaries suffice. In the bond (Peirce/PAL) sense, 'everything is built from teridentities and dyads' holds on infinite domains (Koshkin 2024 Thm 14) and on finite domains with \|D\| ≥ 3. It fails on 2-element domains, where ¬I3 is not a bond of binaries and teridentities, although arities ≥ 4 still reduce to ternaries there. 'Subcubic hypergraph networks' should read subcubic graphs. The register's note that 'Koshkin reports reduction to triads fails on finite domains' is refuted. [koshkin2025completeness] 'PAL proves there is no primitive fourthness' holds only in a stated setting. Koshkin reports that reduction to triads fails on finite domains without hypostatic abstraction. |
| TR-S4-036 | D1 | citation | Abstract: Burch 1991 and Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2006 vindicated Peirce within closed relational clones. | Burch 1991; Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2006 | corrected | → The vindication consists of PAL theorems: Burch 1991 with his restriction, HCP 2006 and HP 2011 without it. They are not proofs 'within closed relational clones', since every relational clone contains =3. 037 (via teridentity's irreducibility in PAL) is right. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] 'Within closed relational clones' is inaccurate: the setting is PAL. In a relational clone, =3 is present automatically. |
| TR-S4-037 | D1 | logical | Vindication came by demonstrating teridentity's irreducibility. |  | corrected | → The vindication consists of PAL theorems: Burch 1991 with his restriction, HCP 2006 and HP 2011 without it. They are not proofs 'within closed relational clones', since every relational clone contains =3. 037 (via teridentity's irreducibility in PAL) is right. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] |

| TR-S4-041 | D1 | citation | Burch (1991) proved the Reduction Thesis; Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2006) and Koshkin (2024) gave generalized proofs. | Burch 1991; Hereth Correia and Pöschel 2006; Koshkin 2024 | corrected | → Koshkin's proofs are (i) a derivation of the bond thesis on infinite domains as a corollary of projoin results (IGPL 2023/24, Thm 14) and (ii) a uniform proof for finite domains by pp-composition and functional completeness (IGPL 2025). Neither uses PAL. The TCSPS 2022 paper argues that the thesis is NOT gerrymandered once operations are explicated, so the register's 'argues the thesis is setting-dependent' inverts it. The Burch and HP halves belong to other clusters. [koshkin2024reduction] Koshkin gives a generalized proof in a different framework (joins and projoins) and argues that the thesis is setting-dependent ('gerrymandered', PhilSci-Archive 23543). |
| TR-S4-042 | D1 | logical | Teridentity cannot be generated from monadic and dyadic relations when \|A\| >= 2. |  | corrected | → True in PAL without teridentity for \|A\|≥2, with no domain expansion (HCP 2006 Thm 2). It is not a theorem of 'relational clone theory' (045): in pp closure =3 = x=y ∧ y=z. For Burch (090) it holds only under the juxtaposition restriction. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] True in PAL without branching, false under pp-definability (x=y ∧ y=z). Name the operations. |

Conarroe (p. 138) independently transcribes the rule as μ+ν−2λ. Cite Herzberger 1981, pp. 41–58 (start page confirmed by the ToC). The lab half of D4's sentence is supported: org_frontier/thinkers/peirce/FINDINGS.md H1 row 'post hoc: max cause-side adicity 2 in all 89'; paper.md: 'Herzberger's valency argument holds without exception across all 89 wirings'. A Burch 1997 OCR snippet lists COMMA first among the PAL operators (COMMA, QUANT, HOOKID, PRODUCT). | TR-S4-065 | D5 | historical | The reduction thesis is formalized in Peircean Algebraic Logic (PAL) and in relational clone theory by Burch, Hereth Correia, Pöschel and Koshkin. |  | corrected | → Koshkin did not develop universal algebra, PAL or relational clone theory. He credits co-clones to Post and the Pol-Inv tradition, and PAL to Burch. In the 2023/24 paper he unifies database joins, the co-clone (projoin) algebra and Peirce's bond algebra, and says he avoids the Pol-Inv Galois connection. Only the 2025 paper works through functional completeness and co-clone bases. Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2004 connected PAL to clones. 'Dau (2006)' is Hereth Correia & Dau 2006, LNCS 3874. The Hereth Correia, Pöschel and Dau rows belong to other clusters. [koshkin2024reduction] Relational clone theory is not their formalization of the thesis. The formalization is PAL, compared with clones by Hereth Correia & Pöschel. |

| TR-S4-070 | D5 | logical | The relational clone generated by all monadic and dyadic relations over any domain with \|A\| >= 2, <Rel^(<=2)(A)>_pp, is strictly incomplete. |  | refuted | → =3 ∈ ⟨Rel^(≤2)(A)⟩_pp for every A. Every relational algebra contains all diagonal relations (⟨∅⟩_RA = D_A, HCP 2004 §3), and =3 = ∆(ζ(= × =)) (Dau & HC p.108). The true theorem is id3 ∉ ⟨Rel^(≤2)(A)⟩_PAL∖{id3}, PAL without teridentity, which allows products, two-place joins, complement and permutation but not coordinate identification (HCP 2006 Thm 2, \|A\|≥2; HCP 2004 Thm 13 for finite A). 'Restricted pp logic' (109) should be stated as PAL without teridentity. [dau2006instances] Likely false as stated. In pp semantics =3 is x=y ∧ y=z, and for \|A\| >= 3 all binary relations pp-generate Rel(A). Restate it as a PAL theorem. |
| TR-S4-071 | D5 | logical | Teridentity =_3 is not in the primitive-positive clone generated by relations of arity <= 2. |  | refuted | → =3 ∈ ⟨Rel^(≤2)(A)⟩_pp for every A. Every relational algebra contains all diagonal relations (⟨∅⟩_RA = D_A, HCP 2004 §3), and =3 = ∆(ζ(= × =)) (Dau & HC p.108). The true theorem is id3 ∉ ⟨Rel^(≤2)(A)⟩_PAL∖{id3}, PAL without teridentity, which allows products, two-place joins, complement and permutation but not coordinate identification (HCP 2006 Thm 2, \|A\|≥2; HCP 2004 Thm 13 for finite A). 'Restricted pp logic' (109) should be stated as PAL without teridentity. [dau2006instances] Likely false in standard pp semantics: every co-clone contains all diagonal relations. |
| TR-S4-072 | D6 | argument_step | Relational clone theory and Peircean Algebraic Logic (PAL) were formalized by Burch, Hereth Correia, Pöschel, Dau and Koshkin. |  | corrected | → Koshkin did not develop universal algebra, PAL or relational clone theory. He credits co-clones to Post and the Pol-Inv tradition, and PAL to Burch. In the 2023/24 paper he unifies database joins, the co-clone (projoin) algebra and Peirce's bond algebra, and says he avoids the Pol-Inv Galois connection. Only the 2025 paper works through functional completeness and co-clone bases. Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2004 connected PAL to clones. 'Dau (2006)' is Hereth Correia & Dau 2006, LNCS 3874. The Hereth Correia, Pöschel and Dau rows belong to other clusters. [koshkin2024reduction] |
| TR-S4-073 | D6 | logical | Clone theory and PAL demonstrate that Quine's reduction is invalid in any closed relational universe A with \|A\| >= 2. |  | refuted | → Keep the existing correction. The closest published position to a 'Quinean Fallacy' is Koshkin 2022: pairing reductions taken 'in anything other than a narrow technical sense' are 'instances of the use/mention error' (p. 2), while Quine is 'correct in a technical algebraic sense' (p. 4). No one proves the reduction 'fails' or is 'invalid'. [quine1954reduction] 'Invalid' is wrong: Quine's reduction is a valid theorem that expands the domain. The PAL results do not refute it. |

| TR-S4-079 | D6 | logical | Relational clone theory shows the pairing operation is itself an irreducible triadic relation linking both coordinates and the pair. |  | corrected | → No source shows in clone theory that the pairing operation is an irreducible triad. In an EG/FOL explication, Koshkin 2022 shows that the pairing REDUCTION is a triple junction ∃t[R1(t,x1) ∧ R2(t,x2) ∧ R̂(t,x3)] which explicates to a teridentity. As a ternary relation, a pairing graph is bond-irreducible (it is non-degenerate) but pp-reducible to binaries on infinite domains. [koshkin2022reduction] Pair(x1,x2,p) is ternary. Whether it is PAL-irreducible is not shown in any source D6 gives. This may be Gemini's inference. |

| TR-S4-081 | D6 | logical | Over a fixed domain in primitive positive logic, the clone generated by all monadic and dyadic relations is strictly incomplete. |  | refuted | → =3 ∈ ⟨Rel^(≤2)(A)⟩_pp for every A. Every relational algebra contains all diagonal relations (⟨∅⟩_RA = D_A, HCP 2004 §3), and =3 = ∆(ζ(= × =)) (Dau & HC p.108). The true theorem is id3 ∉ ⟨Rel^(≤2)(A)⟩_PAL∖{id3}, PAL without teridentity, which allows products, two-place joins, complement and permutation but not coordinate identification (HCP 2006 Thm 2, \|A\|≥2; HCP 2004 Thm 13 for finite A). 'Restricted pp logic' (109) should be stated as PAL without teridentity. [dau2006instances] Likely false in pp semantics; see row 014. |
| TR-S4-082 | D6 | logical | Teridentity =_3 is not in the pp-clone generated by all relations of arity <= 2 on A. |  | refuted | → =3 ∈ ⟨Rel^(≤2)(A)⟩_pp for every A. Every relational algebra contains all diagonal relations (⟨∅⟩_RA = D_A, HCP 2004 §3), and =3 = ∆(ζ(= × =)) (Dau & HC p.108). The true theorem is id3 ∉ ⟨Rel^(≤2)(A)⟩_PAL∖{id3}, PAL without teridentity, which allows products, two-place joins, complement and permutation but not coordinate identification (HCP 2006 Thm 2, \|A\|≥2; HCP 2004 Thm 13 for finite A). 'Restricted pp logic' (109) should be stated as PAL without teridentity. [dau2006instances] Likely false in pp semantics: =3 is x=y ∧ y=z. |

It is not a theorem of 'relational clone theory' (045): in pp closure =3 = x=y ∧ y=z. For Burch (090) it holds only under the juxtaposition restriction. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] True in PAL without branching; state the operations. |
| TR-S4-089 | G1 t1 | argument_step | The impossibility holds only absent model/domain expansion; expanding the domain is the escape route (implied). |  | corrected | → Domain extension is the escape route only for projoin/pp reduction to binaries via hypostatic abstraction of large relations on finite domains. Every relation is small on an infinite domain, which assumes the axiom of choice. Bond irreducibility of teridentity holds on every domain with ≥ 2 elements whatever the extension. For \|D\| ≥ 3, pp-reduction to binaries succeeds with no extension. [koshkin2025completeness] Implied, not stated, in G1. |
| TR-S4-090 | G1 t1 | citation | Cites Burch 1991 for the non-reducibility of triads over a fixed domain. | Burch 1991 | corrected | → True in PAL without teridentity for \|A\|≥2, with no domain expansion (HCP 2006 Thm 2). It is not a theorem of 'relational clone theory' (045): in pp closure =3 = x=y ∧ y=z. For Burch (090) it holds only under the juxtaposition restriction. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] Burch's proof carries a juxtaposition restriction (rows 142/200). |
| TR-S4-091 | G1 t1 | citation | Cites Hereth Correia & Pöschel (no year given) for the non-reducibility result. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (no year given) | corrected | → Koshkin can be cited for bond/positive irreducibility. He proves every non-degenerate ternary bond-irreducible (2023/24 Thm 16) and gives an elementary proof for I3 (2025 Thm 6). With negation allowed on finite domains, the irreducibility result belongs to Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2006 and Hereth & Pöschel 2011, as Koshkin says. In the pp/co-clone sense I3 is reducible, since I3 = (x=y) ∧ (y=z). The register note ('mainly the reduction clause') undersells Koshkin's irreducibility proofs. [koshkin2025completeness] No year given; pick the paper. |
| TR-S4-092 | G1 t1 | citation | Cites Koshkin 2024 for the non-reducibility result. | Koshkin 2024 | corrected | → Koshkin can be cited for bond/positive irreducibility. He proves every non-degenerate ternary bond-irreducible (2023/24 Thm 16) and gives an elementary proof for I3 (2025 Thm 6). With negation allowed on finite domains, the irreducibility result belongs to Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2006 and Hereth & Pöschel 2011, as Koshkin says. In the pp/co-clone sense I3 is reducible, since I3 = (x=y) ∧ (y=z). The register note ('mainly the reduction clause') undersells Koshkin's irreducibility proofs. [koshkin2025completeness] Koshkin's contribution is mainly the reduction clause and its dependence on domain, not the non-reducibility of triads. |
| TR-S4-093 | G1 t1 | logical | Teridentity is join-irreducible (relational-clone sense). |  | refuted | → 'Join-irreducible' has 0 hits in HCP 2004, HCP 2006, Dau & HC 2006, HP 2011 and the three Koshkin papers. In the lattice of relational clones, ⟨{=3}⟩_RA = ⟨∅⟩_RA = D_A (HCP 2004 §3) is the bottom element, so the lattice term does not apply. The confusion is probably with PAL's operation named 'join' (δ^{i,j}). The theorem is that id3 cannot be built by PAL operations, joins included, from unary and binary relations without id3 (HCP 2006 Thm 2). [herethcorreia2006teridentity] Likely a Gemini term that is misapplied; see row 003. |
| TR-S4-094 | G1 t1 | logical | Polyadic relations are formed via subcubic hypergraph networks. |  | corrected | → In the co-clone (primitive positive) sense, relations of arity ≤ 3 generate all relations on every domain (Koshkin 2025, Thm 2). On a finite domain one ternary Sheffer relation suffices, and for \|D\| ≥ 3 binaries suffice. In the bond (Peirce/PAL) sense, 'everything is built from teridentities and dyads' holds on infinite domains (Koshkin 2024 Thm 14) and on finite domains with \|D\| ≥ 3. It fails on 2-element domains, where ¬I3 is not a bond of binaries and teridentities, although arities ≥ 4 still reduce to ternaries there. 'Subcubic hypergraph networks' should read subcubic graphs. The register's note that 'Koshkin reports reduction to triads fails on finite domains' is refuted. [koshkin2025completeness] 'Subcubic hypergraph networks' is Gemini's phrasing. On finite domains Koshkin reports that reduction to triads fails. |

(1991), A Peircean Reduction Thesis: The Foundations of Topological Logic, Lubbock: Texas Tech University Press. | TR-S4-097 | G6 t1 | historical | Burch (1991) is the foundational text that established PAL and proved ternary relations irreducible under specific clone restrictions. | Burch, Robert W. (1991). A Peircean Reduction Thesis | corrected | → As reported by HCP 2006 and Dau & HC 2006 (Burch 1991 not read), Burch proved the thesis in PAL under a juxtaposition restriction, which is not a 'clone restriction'. Burch's own SEP summary says the proof was 'accomplished in 1988 (Burch 1991)' and omits the restriction. 'First algebraic proof' (132, 199) is wrong. Herzberger 1981 proves T7 (lower polyads absolutely irreducible) and T8 (the reduction thesis holds within any sufficiently large domain) in his bonding algebra; both are matched in two scans. Conarroe reports that Burch 1997 p. 251 claims to drop Herzberger's domain-size stipulation (unread). [burch1991reduction] 'Under specific clone restrictions' is wrong: the restriction is on PAL juxtaposition. |

[koshkin2024reduction] At most this confirms the reduction clause (arity >= 4 reduces to 3) on infinite domains, not the irreducibility of triads. |

The true theorem is id3 ∉ ⟨Rel^(≤2)(A)⟩_PAL∖{id3}, PAL without teridentity, which allows products, two-place joins, complement and permutation but not coordinate identification (HCP 2006 Thm 2, \|A\|≥2; HCP 2004 Thm 13 for finite A). 'Restricted pp logic' (109) should be stated as PAL without teridentity. [dau2006instances] 'Restricted pp logic' is unclear. Unrestricted pp-definability gives =3 at once (x=y ∧ y=z). |

| TR-S4-119 | G6 t4 | definition | Koshkin introduces 'ternarity' as a measure of relational complexity. | Koshkin (2024) | corrected | → The term and definition are Koshkin's. He first introduced 'positive ternarity' in TCSPS 2022 as a measure of genuine triadicity or Thirdness, and formalized it in IGPL 2023/24 as the minimal number of ternaries in a bond reduction to arity ≤ 3. It measures 'complexity of relating'. It does not measure 'non-factorizability', because it is finite exactly when the relation factors down to arity ≤ 3. [koshkin2024reduction] Confirm that the term and definition are Koshkin's. |
| TR-S4-120 | G6 t4 | logical | Projective joins (projoins) allow hypostatic abstraction. | Koshkin (2024) | corrected | → TR-S4-120 is verified: hypostatic abstraction is a projoin (Thm 9). TR-S4-123 overstates. Bonds are a restricted subclass of projoins, in which no attribute is shared by three or more factors ('more or less, Peirce’s project-join algebra'). Bond and projoin reducibility coincide for n ≥ 4 and diverge on ternaries (Thm 13). [koshkin2024reduction] |
| TR-S4-121 | G6 t4 | logical | On infinite domains every relation admits a 1-key. | Koshkin (2024) | verified | → Verified. The result rests on \|D\|^n = \|D\|, i.e. the axiom of choice (p. 15). [koshkin2024reduction] |
| TR-S4-122 | G6 t4 | logical | Premise 1: traditional join reductions leave many relations irreducible. | Koshkin (2024) | verified | → Verified. The share of join-reducible relations goes to 0 as \|D\| → ∞, not as n → ∞ on a fixed domain. [koshkin2024reduction] |
| TR-S4-123 | G6 t4 | logical | Projoins correspond directly to Peirce's bonding algebra. | Koshkin (2024) | corrected | → TR-S4-120 is verified: hypostatic abstraction is a projoin (Thm 9). TR-S4-123 overstates. Bonds are a restricted subclass of projoins, in which no attribute is shared by three or more factors ('more or less, Peirce’s project-join algebra'). Bond and projoin reducibility coincide for n ≥ 4 and diverge on ternaries (Thm 13). [koshkin2024reduction] |
| TR-S4-124 | G6 t4 | logical | On infinite domains hypostatic abstraction reduces higher-arity relations to ternary ones. | Koshkin (2024) | corrected | → Theorem 14 confirms BOTH clauses in bond form on infinite domains, so the register note is wrong. Hypostatic abstraction reduces higher relations to binaries under projoin (Cor 1). Binaries plus teridentities appear only after bond explication; the reduction is not 'to ternary ones' in general. [koshkin2024reduction] |
| TR-S4-125 | G6 t4 | logical | The hypostatic-abstraction reduction to ternaries fails on finite domains. | Koshkin (2024) | corrected | → On finite domains two things fail: one-parameter hypostatic abstraction for large relations (\|R\| > \|D\|), and the equality ter = n − 2 (Herzberger's quaternary on 3 elements has ternarity 4). Reduction to ternaries itself was left open in 2023/24 (Problem 1, with the author expecting it to succeed) and was proved in 2025 on every domain. Reduction to dyads fails in the pp sense only on 2-element domains. In the bond sense it fails on all domains. [koshkin2024reduction] This is the talk-relevant qualification of Peirce's constructive clause. |
| TR-S4-126 | G6 t4 | argument_step | Conclusion: Peirce's reduction thesis needs refinement via ternarity; finite domains obstruct universal logical factorization. | Koshkin (2024) | corrected | → On finite domains two things fail: one-parameter hypostatic abstraction for large relations (\|R\| > \|D\|), and the equality ter = n − 2 (Herzberger's quaternary on 3 elements has ternarity 4). Reduction to ternaries itself was left open in 2023/24 (Problem 1, with the author expecting it to succeed) and was proved in 2025 on every domain. Reduction to dyads fails in the pp sense only on 2-element domains. In the bond sense it fails on all domains. [koshkin2024reduction] |
| TR-S4-127 | G6 t4 | argument_step | Koshkin proves finite-domain irreducibility mainly by asymptotic counting arguments. | Koshkin (2024) | corrected | → The counting theorems show that almost all relations are irreducible under RESTRICTED means: Cartesian product, join, projoin with a bounded number of parameters, and bonds of bounded ternarity. They show that relations of arbitrarily high ternarity exist. They do not show that relations irreducible under unrestricted projoin or bond exist, as the author says. [koshkin2024reduction] |
| TR-S4-128 | G6 t4 | argument_step | Koshkin's counting shows irreducible relations exist but gives little constructive guidance for specific finite-domain cases. | Koshkin (2024) | corrected | → The counting theorems show that almost all relations are irreducible under RESTRICTED means: Cartesian product, join, projoin with a bounded number of parameters, and bonds of bounded ternarity. They show that relations of arbitrarily high ternarity exist. They do not show that relations irreducible under unrestricted projoin or bond exist, as the author says. [koshkin2024reduction] An evaluative gloss from the G6 review. |
| TR-S4-129 | G6 t5 | citation | Burch (1991) is cited in Koshkin's bibliography and in Hereth Correia & Pöschel's references. | Burch (1991) | verified | → Burch 1991 (A Peircean Reduction Thesis, Texas Tech UP) is in all three Koshkin bibliographies. Hereth Correia & Pöschel 2004 also cites [Bur91] (another cluster's card). [koshkin2024reduction] |
| TR-S4-130 | G6 t5 | historical | Dau & Hereth Correia (2006) and Koshkin (2024) evaluate Burch's work. | Dau & Hereth Correia (2006); Koshkin (2024) | corrected | → Koshkin evaluates Burch in the TCSPS 2022 paper (p. 8: 'the shadow of gerrymander still looms over it'). The 2023/24 IGPL paper follows Burch's derivation and does not evaluate it. 'Dau & Hereth Correia (2006)' is the correct published author order (Crossref doi 10.1007/11671404_7: Dau first; dau2006instances). Koshkin's lists reverse it: 'J. Hereth Correia and F. Dau' (2022, p. 24; in-text pp. 2, 9, 17) and '[13] J. Hereth Correia, F. Dau … 106-118' (2023/24, p. 30, which also has the pages wrong; the volume gives 105-118). Dau & Hereth Correia discuss Burch's juxtaposition restriction (p. 106). The previous correction ('is Hereth Correia & Dau 2006') was backwards. [koshkin2022reduction] |
| TR-S4-131 | G6 t5 | historical | Burch's 1991 monograph introduced PAL and formalized the teridentity relation (=3). | Burch (1991) | corrected | → Reported by HCP 2004, Dau & HC 2006 and Conarroe 2020, and not read in Burch: Burch 1991 algebraized Beta EGs as PAL, with teridentity central. The title correction stands: the book is 'A Peircean Reduction Thesis'; PAL is the system (198). PAL is not 'modern relational clone theory' (066). [burch1991reduction] |
| TR-S4-132 | G6 t5 | historical | Burch (1991) gave the first algebraic proof of the Peircean Reduction Thesis, under restrictive juxtaposition. | Burch (1991) | corrected | → As reported by HCP 2006 and Dau & HC 2006 (Burch 1991 not read), Burch proved the thesis in PAL under a juxtaposition restriction, which is not a 'clone restriction'. Burch's own SEP summary says the proof was 'accomplished in 1988 (Burch 1991)' and omits the restriction. 'First algebraic proof' (132, 199) is wrong. Herzberger 1981 proves T7 (lower polyads absolutely irreducible) and T8 (the reduction thesis holds within any sufficiently large domain) in his bonding algebra; both are matched in two scans. Conarroe reports that Burch 1997 p. 251 claims to drop Herzberger's domain-size stipulation (unread). [burch1991reduction] 'First algebraic proof' is questionable given Herzberger 1981's bonding algebra. |
| TR-S4-133 | G6 t5 | historical | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004) is the predecessor of their 2006 teridentity paper. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004) | verified | → HCP 2006 builds on HCP 2004's restriction-free PAL and cites it for the constructive part and for the graph notation. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] |
| TR-S4-134 | G6 t5 | historical | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004) analyze the expressive power of PAL and existential graphs relative to first-order logic and relational clones. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004) | verified | → Positive PAL corresponds to relational algebra (Pöschel–Kalužnin sense), locally negated PAL to ⟨Q∪¬Q⟩_RA and full PAL to the Krasner algebra. First-order logic enters through Theorem 6. [herethcorreia2004power] |
| TR-S4-135 | G6 t5 | logical | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2006) prove the irreducibility of teridentity without Burch's artificial restrictions. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2006) | verified | → For \|A\|≥2 and Σ = all unary and binary relations, ⟨Σ⟩_PAL∖{id3} ⊊ ⟨Σ⟩_PAL, with unrestricted products and complement. Joins identify exactly two places. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] |
| TR-S4-136 | G6 t5 | citation | Abramsky & Brandenburger (2011), 'The Sheaf-Theoretic Structure of Contextuality'. | Abramsky, S. & Brandenburger, A. (2011). The Sheaf-Theoretic Structure of Contextuality | corrected | → Abramsky, S. & Brandenburger, A. (2011), 'The sheaf-theoretic structure of non-locality and contextuality', New J. Phys. 13(11): 113036, doi 10.1088/1367-2630/13/11/113036. TR-S4-052 is right; TR-S4-026 and -136 drop 'non-locality and'. The card now exists (S5, peripheral). [abramsky2011structure] The title omits 'Non-Locality'. |
| TR-S4-137 | G6 t5 | citation | Abramsky, Barbosa & Searle (2024), 'Combining Contextuality and Causality'. | Abramsky, S., Barbosa, R. S., & Searle, A. (2024). Combining Contextuality and Causality | corrected | → Abramsky, S., Barbosa, R. S. & Searle, A. (2024), 'Combining contextuality and causality: a game semantics approach', Phil. Trans. R. Soc. A 382(2268): 20230002, doi 10.1098/rsta.2023.0002 (CC BY). TR-S4-027 and -058 are right; TR-S4-137 drops the subtitle. [abramsky2024contextuality] [metadata only: the work exists as cited; its text was not read] |
| TR-S4-138 | G6 t5 | historical | Abramsky et al. establish that quantum contextuality and non-locality are topological obstructions to global sections. | Abramsky & Brandenburger (2011); Abramsky, Barbosa & Searle (2024) | corrected | → Verified in substance: contextuality and non-locality 'correspond exactly to obstructions to the existence of global sections' (A&B 2011 abstract; Theorem 8.1). ABS 2024 carries the same criterion over to a presheaf of strategies (p. 8). 'Topological obstruction' is not A&B's phrase. The cohomological version is Abramsky, Mansfield & Barbosa 2012, which A&B report on p. 29. TR-S4-183's 'in quantum systems' overstates the result: specific models (Bell, Hardy, GHZ) lack global sections, not every quantum model. [abramsky2011structure] 'Topological obstruction' is loose; A&B frame it sheaf-theoretically. Peripheral. |
| TR-S4-139 | G6 t5 | argument_step | The sheaf-theoretic obstructions directly mirror relational non-reducibility in hypergraph categories. | Abramsky & Brandenburger (2011) | unverifiable | → Neither Abramsky paper states this bridge. A&B's 'factorizable' concerns hidden-variable models (Theorem 8.1), not decomposition of a relation into dyadic relations, and neither paper mentions hypergraphs. Present it as the talk's analogy only. [abramsky2011structure] Gemini's own bridge claim ('directly mirror'). Avoid it, or treat it as the talk's analogy. |
| TR-S4-140 | G6 t6 | historical | Burch formalizes Peirce's Beta Existential Graphs as an algebra called Peircean Algebraic Logic (PAL). | Burch (1991) | corrected | → Reported by HCP 2004, Dau & HC 2006 and Conarroe 2020, and not read in Burch: Burch 1991 algebraized Beta EGs as PAL, with teridentity central. The title correction stands: the book is 'A Peircean Reduction Thesis'; PAL is the system (198). PAL is not 'modern relational clone theory' (066). [burch1991reduction] |
| TR-S4-141 | G6 t6 | historical | Burch (1991) gives a mathematical proof of Peirce's Reduction Thesis. | Burch (1991) | corrected | → As reported by HCP 2006 and Dau & HC 2006 (Burch 1991 not read), Burch proved the thesis in PAL under a juxtaposition restriction, which is not a 'clone restriction'. Burch's own SEP summary says the proof was 'accomplished in 1988 (Burch 1991)' and omits the restriction. 'First algebraic proof' (132, 199) is wrong. Herzberger 1981 proves T7 (lower polyads absolutely irreducible) and T8 (the reduction thesis holds within any sufficiently large domain) in his bonding algebra; both are matched in two scans. Conarroe reports that Burch 1997 p. 251 claims to drop Herzberger's domain-size stipulation (unread). [burch1991reduction] |
| TR-S4-142 | G6 t6 | logical | To make the theorem tractable, Burch permits juxtaposition (Cartesian product) only at the terminal or penultimate steps of term construction. | Burch (1991) | corrected | → Reported by three PAL papers, which agree with one another, and not read in Burch: juxtaposition is allowed only 'as last or before last operation', via join2 = δ(ρ×σ). 145's 'artificially restricting intermediate branchings' is the register's gloss; Dau & HC p.106 report that Burch proves expressivity unchanged. Burch's SEP entry does not mention the restriction and leaves juxtaposition off its list of Peirce's resources. [burch1991reduction] G6 attributes this to an unidentified EBSCO PDF. |
| TR-S4-143 | G6 t6 | definition | Burch treats relations as primitive 'relations-simpliciter' grounded in graph topology, not as nominalist classes of tuples. | Burch (1991) | unverifiable | → The book was not read. Conarroe transcribes APRT p. ix: 'Peirce was certainly some sort of realist with regard to relations simpliciter…relations as such were fundamental, whereas individual entities were derivative by means of (hypostatic) abstraction'. If accurate, the phrase is Burch's description of Peirce's view, not a stated treatment of PAL relations. 'Grounded in graph topology' is not attested. [burch1991reduction] From the unidentified EBSCO PDF (SOURCE.md's top open question for S4). |
| TR-S4-144 | G6 t6 | definition | In Burch, relational composition is governed by topological invariants: valence, edge count, vertex count, choresis, cyclosis. | Burch (1991) | unverifiable | → Not read. The 1992 abstract confirms that Burch analyses 'valental' characteristics ('valence' = number of argument-places) and offers PAL as 'a bridge between logic and topological graph theory'. Edge and vertex counts and Listing's choresis/cyclosis are unchecked. [burch1991reduction] 'Choresis' and 'cyclosis' are Listing's terms. Verify that Burch uses them. |
| TR-S4-145 | G6 t6 | logical | Burch's juxtaposition operator is confined to terminal steps, restricting intermediate relational branchings. | Burch (1991) | corrected | → Reported by three PAL papers, which agree with one another, and not read in Burch: juxtaposition is allowed only 'as last or before last operation', via join2 = δ(ρ×σ). 145's 'artificially restricting intermediate branchings' is the register's gloss; Dau & HC p.106 report that Burch proves expressivity unchanged. Burch's SEP entry does not mention the restriction and leaves juxtaposition off its list of Peirce's resources. [burch1991reduction] |
| TR-S4-146 | G6 t6 | logical | Translating standard quantification theory into PAL graphs requires hypostatic (higher-order) abstraction. | Burch (1991) | corrected | → Reported by HCP 2004 Remark 15: Burch uses hypostatic abstraction, i.e. domain extension, for the constructive clause on finite domains. A single OCR snippet of Burch 1997 fits this ('The full rationale for the positive part of Peirce's reduction thesis involves the notion of “hypostatic…'). That it is needed to translate quantification theory into PAL is not attested. [burch1991reduction] |
| TR-S4-147 | G6 t6 | argument_step | Burch's terminal-juxtaposition restriction undermines the universality of his proof. | Burch (1991) | corrected | → The restriction limits Burch's proof, not PAL's expressivity. Removing it breaks his proof (HCP 2004). The PAL critics' objection is that it makes PAL unlike EGs; the 'undermines universality' charge is Skidmore/Conarroe's gerrymandering worry. 149: in unrestricted relational algebra =3 is trivially constructible. The open question was unrestricted PAL without teridentity. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] An evaluative claim; attribute it to its critics if they make it. |
| TR-S4-148 | G6 t6 | argument_step | Prohibiting intermediate Cartesian products artificially blocks complex intermediate coordinate networks. | Burch (1991) | corrected | → The restriction limits Burch's proof, not PAL's expressivity. Removing it breaks his proof (HCP 2004). The PAL critics' objection is that it makes PAL unlike EGs; the 'undermines universality' charge is Skidmore/Conarroe's gerrymandering worry. 149: in unrestricted relational algebra =3 is trivially constructible. The open question was unrestricted PAL without teridentity. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] |
| TR-S4-149 | G6 t6 | argument_step | Burch's restriction leaves open whether an unrestricted relational algebra could build =3 by interleaving joins and products. | Burch (1991) | corrected | → The restriction limits Burch's proof, not PAL's expressivity. Removing it breaks his proof (HCP 2004). The PAL critics' objection is that it makes PAL unlike EGs; the 'undermines universality' charge is Skidmore/Conarroe's gerrymandering worry. 149: in unrestricted relational algebra =3 is trivially constructible. The open question was unrestricted PAL without teridentity. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] |

These are PAL proofs, related to relational clones, not 'relational clone proofs'. [herethcorreia2004power] |
| TR-S4-151 | G6 t6 | historical | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004) compare PAL's expressive power with relational algebras, Krasner algebras and first-order logic. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004) | verified | → Positive PAL corresponds to relational algebra (Pöschel–Kalužnin sense), locally negated PAL to ⟨Q∪¬Q⟩_RA and full PAL to the Krasner algebra. First-order logic enters through Theorem 6. [herethcorreia2004power] |
| TR-S4-152 | G6 t6 | logical | Without Burch's restriction, unrestricted positive PAL is functionally equivalent to classical relational algebra. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004) | corrected | → Positive PAL = relational algebra ⟨Q⟩_RA in the Pöschel–Kalužnin sense, i.e. pp closure (Thm 9(i)), not Tarski's relation algebra. 156 is refuted: PAL with negation equals the Krasner algebra (Thm 9(iii)), and neither equals 'Tarskian relational algebra'. [herethcorreia2004power] 'Classical relational algebra' is ambiguous (Codd or Tarski). Check which one. |
| TR-S4-153 | G6 t6 | logical | Full PAL with negation equals Krasner algebras of the first kind. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004) | corrected | → ⟨Q⟩_PAL = ⟨Q⟩_KA (Krasner algebra = closure under (R1)–(R7)). The paper does not say 'of the first kind'. [herethcorreia2004power] |
| TR-S4-154 | G6 t6 | logical | Teridentity is indispensable: it cannot be generated positively from lower-arity relations on domains with more than one element. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004) | corrected | → Theorem 13 covers finite A with \|A\|≥2, for full and positive PAL without id3. Only the positive case is proved in the paper (Remark 14): a vertex-minimal graph has only pending edges and so evaluates to σ1×σ2×σ3 or ρ×σ, which are Cartesian factorizations. 'Diagonal pattern projections' (159) is not in the text. [herethcorreia2004power] |
| TR-S4-155 | G6 t6 | logical | Relational closures correspond to Krasner algebras via the Pol-Inv and Aut-Inv Galois connections. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004) | corrected | → The paper invokes the Pol–Inv and Aut–Inv Galois connections only in the \|A\|=2 sketch of Prop. 11. Relational algebras (co-clones) correspond to Pol–Inv and Krasner algebras to Aut–Inv, on finite A. [herethcorreia2004power] Standard Galois theory: Pol-Inv for clones and co-clones, Aut-Inv for Krasner algebras. |
| TR-S4-156 | G6 t6 | logical | Unrestricted PAL operations (id3, projection, product, coordinate identification, negation) have the same expressive power as Tarskian relational algebra. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004) | corrected | → Positive PAL = relational algebra ⟨Q⟩_RA in the Pöschel–Kalužnin sense, i.e. pp closure (Thm 9(i)), not Tarski's relation algebra. 156 is refuted: PAL with negation equals the Krasner algebra (Thm 9(iii)), and neither equals 'Tarskian relational algebra'. [herethcorreia2004power] This contradicts rows 152/153: it lists negation among the operations yet equates them with relational algebra, not with Krasner algebras. Check. |
| TR-S4-157 | G6 t6 | definition | Teridentity id3 = {(a,a,a) \| a in A}; it corresponds algebraically to the diagonal relations D_A = <id3>_RA. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004) | corrected | → ⟨∅⟩_RA = ⟨∅⟩_pPAL = D_A, the set of all diagonal relations, which includes id3. id3 generates all diagonals in PAL (Prop. 16). [herethcorreia2004power] |
| TR-S4-158 | G6 t6 | argument_step | Premise 1: generating the full relational clone requires closure under coordinate identification (Δ), which evaluates to δij(ζ(= × =)). | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004) | corrected | → The formula is from Dau & Hereth Correia 2006 p.108, not HCP 2004: =3 = ∆(ζ(= × =)), with ∆ the identification of the first two coordinates (HCP 2004 R3), not δij. It shows =3 in the relational-algebra closure of the binary identity. [dau2006instances] Garbled notation (δij(ζ(= × =))); verify against the source. |
| TR-S4-159 | G6 t6 | logical | Premise 2: positive generation from arity <= 2 relations without id3 yields only trivial Cartesian factorizations or diagonal projections when \|A\| > 1. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004) | corrected | → Theorem 13 covers finite A with \|A\|≥2, for full and positive PAL without id3. Only the positive case is proved in the paper (Remark 14): a vertex-minimal graph has only pending edges and so evaluates to σ1×σ2×σ3 or ρ×σ, which are Cartesian factorizations. 'Diagonal pattern projections' (159) is not in the text. [herethcorreia2004power] Likely a Gemini paraphrase; unverified. |

Only the positive case is proved in the paper (Remark 14): a vertex-minimal graph has only pending edges and so evaluates to σ1×σ2×σ3 or ρ×σ, which are Cartesian factorizations. 'Diagonal pattern projections' (159) is not in the text. [herethcorreia2004power] |

The theorem also claims the full-PAL (negation) case, with its proof deferred. [herethcorreia2004power] |
| TR-S4-162 | G6 t6 | argument_step | After 2004 it remained open whether full Krasner algebras with complementation could construct =3 from binary relations over finite domains. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2004) | corrected | → The negation case was asserted in 2004 but unproved in print until HCP 2006 (and Dau & HC 2006 for infinite and two-element A). The open item was PAL without teridentity, with complement. Full Krasner algebras always contain =3. [herethcorreia2004power] |
| TR-S4-163 | G6 t6 | historical | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2006) give a complete proof of the difficult direction of Peirce's Reduction Thesis. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2006) | verified | → For \|A\|≥2 and Σ = all unary and binary relations, ⟨Σ⟩_PAL∖{id3} ⊊ ⟨Σ⟩_PAL, with unrestricted products and complement. Joins identify exactly two places. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] |
| TR-S4-164 | G6 t6 | logical | Teridentity cannot be built in full PAL from unary and binary relations, even with unrestricted products and negation. | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2006) | verified | → For \|A\|≥2 and Σ = all unary and binary relations, ⟨Σ⟩_PAL∖{id3} ⊊ ⟨Σ⟩_PAL, with unrestricted products and complement. Joins identify exactly two places. [herethcorreia2006teridentity] Keep 'without branching' explicit: PAL's join never identifies more than two places at once. |
| TR-S4-165 | G6 t6 | logical | The 2006 proof uses a canonical union-of-intersections representation (disjunctive normal form over relational intersections). | Hereth Correia & Pöschel (2006) | corrected | → 165 is verified. 167 is garbled. Theorem 1: each id3-free term has a ∪∩-representation whose members have at most two essential places, are comparable or inverted-comparable when their essential places overlap, and are themselves generable without id3.
